  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c01",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The European dragon is a legendary creature in folklore and mythology among the overlapping cultures of Europe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga Eropa adalah makhluk legendaris dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi berbagai budaya Eropa yang saling bertumpang tindih.",
        "en": "The European dragon is a legendary creature of the folklore and mythology of Europe's overlapping cultures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c02",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "In and after the Early Middle Ages, the European dragon is typically depicted as a large, fire-breathing, scaly, horned, lizard-like creature; the creature also has leathery, bat-like wings, and a long, muscular prehensile tail.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak Abad Pertengahan Awal, naga Eropa biasanya digambarkan sebagai makhluk besar mirip kadal yang menyemburkan api, bersisik, bertanduk, bersayap kulit seperti kelelawar, dan berekor panjang berotot yang dapat mencengkeram.",
        "en": "From the Early Middle Ages the European dragon is usually shown as a large, fire-breathing, scaly, horned, lizard-like creature with leathery bat-like wings and a long, muscular, grasping tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c03",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The typical dragon in Christian culture protects a cavern or castle filled with gold and treasure. An evil dragon is often associated with a great hero who tries to slay it, and a good one is said to give support or wise advice.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dalam budaya Kristen biasanya menjaga gua atau kastel berisi emas dan harta; naga jahat sering dikaitkan dengan pahlawan yang berusaha membunuhnya, sedangkan naga baik memberi bantuan atau nasihat bijak.",
        "en": "The typical dragon of Christian culture guards a cave or castle full of gold and treasure; an evil dragon is tied to a hero who tries to slay it, while a good one gives help or wise counsel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c04",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "In some accounts, the hero Sigurð defeats Fáfnir by digging a pit and then lying in wait, piercing his heart with a sword as he passes overhead and slaying him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian kisah, pahlawan Sigurð mengalahkan Fáfnir dengan menggali lubang, menunggu, lalu menusuk jantungnya dengan pedang ketika naga itu lewat di atasnya.",
        "en": "In some accounts the hero Sigurð defeats Fáfnir by digging a pit, lying in wait and stabbing his heart with a sword as he passes over."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c05",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The Roman poet Virgil in his poem Culex lines 163–201, describing a shepherd battling a big constricting snake, calls it \"serpens\" and also \"draco\", showing that in his time the two words probably could mean the same thing.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyair Romawi Vergilius dalam puisi Culex (baris 163–201) menyebut ular besar yang dilawan seorang gembala dengan kata serpens sekaligus draco, menunjukkan kedua kata itu mungkin bermakna sama pada masanya.",
        "en": "The Roman poet Virgil, in his poem Culex (lines 163–201), calls the great snake a shepherd fights both serpens and draco, showing the two words could probably mean the same thing in his day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c06",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "Roman dragons developed from serpentine Greek ones, combined with the dragons of the Near East, in the context of the hybrid Greek/Eastern Hellenistic culture.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga Romawi berkembang dari naga Yunani yang berwujud ular, berpadu dengan naga Timur Dekat dalam budaya Helenistik.",
        "en": "Roman dragons grew out of serpent-like Greek ones combined with Near Eastern dragons in the hybrid Hellenistic culture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c07",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "Le dragon (du grec ancien δράκων / drákōn, du verbe δέρκομαι / dérkomai, « voir, percer du regard », et du latin draco) est, dans la tradition européenne, une créature légendaire ailée possédant une queue de saurien et dont les pattes se terminent par des griffes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, kata dragon berasal dari bahasa Yunani Kuno drákōn, dari kata kerja dérkomai, \"melihat, menatap tajam\", dan bahasa Latin draco; dalam tradisi Eropa ia makhluk bersayap berekor kadal dan berkaki bercakar.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the word dragon comes from Ancient Greek drákōn, from dérkomai, 'to see, to stare', and Latin draco; in European tradition it is a winged creature with a lizard's tail and clawed feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c08",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "On considère souvent comme des dragons plusieurs créatures de la mythologie grecque : Python, Ladon, l'Hydre de Lerne, etc. Mais ceux-ci ne sont en réalité représentés sur les œuvres antiques que comme des serpents, parfois gigantesques ou multicéphales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Python, Ladon, dan Hydra dari Lerna sering dianggap naga, tetapi dalam karya kuno mereka hanya digambarkan sebagai ular, kadang raksasa atau berkepala banyak.",
        "en": "Python, Ladon and the Lernaean Hydra are often counted as dragons, but ancient works show them only as serpents, sometimes giant or many-headed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c09",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "Les termes de worm (dans la mythologie germanique), wyrm (en anglo-saxon), ou wurm (en vieux haut allemand) décrivant parfois des créatures apparentées aux dragons signifient par ailleurs serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah worm (mitologi Jermanik), wyrm (Anglo-Saxon), dan wurm (Jerman Tinggi Kuno) untuk makhluk mirip naga juga berarti ular.",
        "en": "The terms worm (Germanic myth), wyrm (Anglo-Saxon) and wurm (Old High German) for dragon-like creatures also mean serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c10",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s3",
      "quote": "四肢とは独立して翼を持つのではなく、鳥やコウモリのように前足が翼になっている場合もあり、そうしたものはワイバーン（英: Wyvern）と呼ばれることが多い。逆に、翼を持たない姿で描かれる場合もあり、こちらはワーム（英: Worm）と呼ばれることがある。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jepang, naga yang kaki depannya menjadi sayap seperti burung atau kelelawar sering disebut wyvern, sedangkan naga tanpa sayap kadang disebut worm.",
        "en": "According to the Japanese article, dragons whose forelegs are wings like a bird's or bat's are often called wyverns, while wingless ones are sometimes called worms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "european-dragon-c11",
      "source_id": "european-dragon-s3",
      "quote": "日本語では竜（りゅう）とも呼ばれ、東洋の竜とはその特徴に類似性が認められるものの、直接の関係はない。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Jepang ia juga disebut ryū; ia mirip naga Timur tetapi tidak berhubungan langsung.",
        "en": "In Japanese it is also called ryū; it resembles the Eastern dragon but is not directly related."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Domba Tumbuhan Tartaria adalah tumbuhan legendaris Asia Tengah yang dipercaya berbuah domba hidup yang tersambung lewat tali pusar, legenda yang berakar pada tanaman kapas.",
    "en": "The Vegetable Lamb of Tartary is a legendary plant of Central Asia believed to bear live sheep tied to it by an umbilical cord, a legend rooted in the cotton plant.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c01",
      "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Domba Tumbuhan Tartaria atau Agnus scythicus adalah zoofit legendaris Asia Tengah yang dipercaya berbuah domba. Domba itu terhubung ke tumbuhan oleh tangkai seperti tali pusar, merumput di sekitarnya, dan mati bila rumput habis. Ia dipercaya berdarah, bertulang, dan berdaging seperti domba; darahnya manis seperti madu, wolnya dipakai untuk pakaian, dan hanya serigala yang memangsanya.",
      "en": "The Vegetable Lamb of Tartary or Agnus scythicus is a legendary zoophyte of Central Asia believed to bear sheep. The lamb, tied to the plant by a cord-like stalk, grazes around it and dies when the grass runs out. It was thought to have blood, bones and flesh like a lamb; its blood was sweet as honey, its wool made into clothes, and only wolves preyed on it.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "id": "Legenda ini berakar pada kapas, yang belum dikenal di Eropa utara; Herodotos sudah menulis tentang pohon berbuah wol di India, dan Yeduah dalam cerita rakyat Yahudi tercatat sejak 436 M. Odorico sekitar 1314 menulis tentang melon yang melahirkan anak domba, Thomas Browne menyebutnya Boramez, dan nama barometz berasal dari kata Tatar untuk anak domba.       "en": "The legend is rooted in cotton, then unknown in northern Europe; Herodotus already wrote of wool-bearing trees in India, and the Yeduah of Jewish folklore is recorded from AD 436.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c01",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "The Vegetable Lamb of Tartary (Latin: Agnus scythicus or Planta Tartarica Barometz) is a legendary zoophyte of Central Asia, once believed to grow sheep as its fruit. It was believed the sheep were connected to the plant by an umbilical cord and grazed the land around the plant. When all accessible foliage was gone, both the plant and sheep died.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Domba Tumbuhan Tartaria (Latin: Agnus scythicus atau Planta Tartarica Barometz) adalah zoofit legendaris Asia Tengah yang dipercaya berbuah domba; domba itu terhubung ke tumbuhan oleh tali pusar dan merumput di sekitarnya, dan bila rumput habis, tumbuhan dan dombanya mati.",
        "en": "The Vegetable Lamb of Tartary (Latin Agnus scythicus or Planta Tartarica Barometz) is a legendary zoophyte of Central Asia believed to bear sheep as fruit; the sheep, tied to the plant by an umbilical cord, grazed around it, and when the foliage was gone both died."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c02",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "Underlying the legend is the cotton plant, which was unknown in Northern Europe before the Norman conquest of Sicily.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini berakar pada tanaman kapas, yang tidak dikenal di Eropa utara sebelum penaklukan Sisilia oleh bangsa Norman.",
        "en": "Behind the legend is the cotton plant, unknown in northern Europe before the Norman conquest of Sicily."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c03",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "Thomas Browne's Pseudodoxia Epidemica named it as the Boramez.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pseudodoxia Epidemica karya Thomas Browne menyebutnya Boramez.",
        "en": "Thomas Browne's Pseudodoxia Epidemica called it the Boramez."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c04",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "The vegetable lamb was believed to have blood, bones, and flesh like that of a normal lamb.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Domba tumbuhan ini dipercaya berdarah, bertulang, dan berdaging seperti domba biasa.",
        "en": "The vegetable lamb was believed to have blood, bones and flesh like an ordinary lamb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c05",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "It could be eaten, once dead, and its blood supposedly tasted sweet like honey. Its wool was said to be used by the native people of its homeland to make head coverings and other articles of clothing. The only carnivorous animals attracted to the lamb plant (other than humans) were wolves.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah mati ia dapat dimakan dan darahnya konon manis seperti madu; wolnya dipakai penduduk setempat untuk penutup kepala dan pakaian, dan selain manusia hanya serigala yang tertarik memangsanya.",
        "en": "Once dead it could be eaten, its blood supposedly sweet as honey; locals made headwear and clothing from its wool, and apart from humans only wolves were drawn to prey on it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c06",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "The Greek historian Herodotus wrote of trees in India \"the fruit whereof is a wool exceeding in beauty and goodness that of sheep. The natives make their clothes of this tree-wool.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejarawan Yunani Herodotos menulis tentang pohon di India yang buahnya berupa wol yang lebih indah dan lebih baik daripada wol domba, yang dipakai penduduk untuk pakaian.",
        "en": "The Greek historian Herodotus wrote of trees in India whose fruit is a wool finer than sheep's, from which the natives make clothes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c07",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s1",
      "quote": "There is mention of a similar plant-animal in Jewish folklore as early as AD 436. This creature, called the Yeduah",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tumbuhan-hewan serupa yang disebut Yeduah sudah disebut dalam cerita rakyat Yahudi sejak tahun 436 M.",
        "en": "A similar plant-animal called the Yeduah appears in Jewish folklore as early as AD 436."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c08",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s2",
      "quote": "In der Renaissance war das Skythische Lamm das Objekt zahlreicher philosophischer und botanischer Texte, die es als Heuristik nutzen, um die Natur besser zu verstehen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, pada masa Renaisans domba Skithia menjadi pokok banyak teks filsafat dan botani yang memakainya untuk lebih memahami alam.",
        "en": "According to the German article, in the Renaissance the Scythian lamb was the subject of many philosophical and botanical texts that used it to understand nature better."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c09",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s2",
      "quote": "Der Beiname barometz bezieht sich auf das tatarische baranetz, was „Lämmchen“ bedeutet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama barometz merujuk kata Tatar baranetz, \"anak domba\".",
        "en": "The epithet barometz refers to the Tatar baranetz, 'little lamb'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-c10",
      "source_id": "vegetable-lamb-of-tartary-s3",
      "quote": "イタリアの宣教師オデリコは、1314年ごろに東方布教の旅行を記録した書物『東洋旅行記』において、カスピ山脈（現在のコーカサス山脈）には一頭の仔羊大の獣が生まれるメロンがあると紹介した。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jepang, misionaris Italia Odorico sekitar 1314 menulis dalam catatan perjalanannya bahwa di Pegunungan Kaspia (kini Kaukasus) ada melon yang melahirkan binatang sebesar anak domba.",
        "en": "According to the Japanese article, the Italian missionary Odoric around 1314 wrote in his travel account that in the Caspian (now Caucasus) mountains there was a melon that bore a beast the size of a lamb."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "The bishop-fish is a legendary 16th-century sea creature shaped like a bishop that, once caught, begged bishops for release, then made the sign of the cross and vanished into the sea.",

    {
      "id": "Ikan uskup atau uskup laut pertama tercatat pada abad ke-16. Menurut legenda ia dibawa kepada Raja Polandia, lalu diperlihatkan kepada para uskup Katolik, memberi isyarat minta dilepaskan, dan setelah dibebaskan membuat tanda salib lalu lenyap ke laut. Seekor lagi konon ditangkap dekat Jerman pada 1531, menolak makan, dan mati tiga hari kemudian; ia digambar dalam Historiae animalium karya Conrad Gesner.",
      "en": "The bishop-fish or sea bishop was first recorded in the 16th century. By legend it was brought to the King of Poland, shown to Catholic bishops, begged by gesture to be freed, and once released made the sign of the cross and vanished.     {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c01",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s1",
      "quote": "The sea bishop or bishop-fish is a legendary creature first recorded in the 16th century. According to legend, it was taken to the King of Poland, who wished to keep it. It was also shown to a group of Catholic bishops, to whom the bishop-fish gestured, appealing to be released. They granted its wish, at which point it made the sign of the cross and disappeared into the sea.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uskup laut atau ikan uskup adalah makhluk legendaris yang pertama tercatat pada abad ke-16; menurut legenda ia dibawa kepada Raja Polandia yang ingin memeliharanya, lalu diperlihatkan kepada para uskup Katolik, memberi isyarat minta dilepaskan, dan setelah dibebaskan membuat tanda salib lalu lenyap ke laut.",
        "en": "The sea bishop or bishop-fish is a legendary creature first recorded in the 16th century; by legend it was brought to the King of Poland, who wished to keep it, shown to Catholic bishops, gestured to be freed, and once released made the sign of the cross and vanished into the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c02",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s1",
      "quote": "Another was supposedly captured in the ocean near Germany in 1531. It refused to eat and died after three days. It was described and pictured in the fourth volume of Conrad Gesner's famous Historiae animalium, published in 1551–1558 and 1587.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seekor lagi konon ditangkap di laut dekat Jerman pada 1531, menolak makan, dan mati setelah tiga hari; ia dideskripsikan dan digambar dalam jilid keempat Historiae animalium karya Conrad Gesner (1551–1558 dan 1587).",
        "en": "Another was supposedly caught in the sea off Germany in 1531, refused to eat and died after three days; it was described and pictured in volume four of Conrad Gesner's Historiae animalium (1551–1558 and 1587)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c03",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s1",
      "quote": "Cryptozoologist Bernard Heuvelmans believed the report was based on the discovery of a large mutilated Grimaldi scaled squid.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kriptozoolog Bernard Heuvelmans menduga laporan itu berasal dari penemuan cumi-cumi bersisik Grimaldi yang besar dan rusak.",
        "en": "The cryptozoologist Bernard Heuvelmans believed the report was based on a large mutilated Grimaldi scaled squid."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c04",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s2",
      "quote": "Ikan uskup lainnya ditangkap di samudera dekat Jerman pada 1531. Ia menolak untuk makan dan mati tiga hari kemudian.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Indonesia juga menyebut ikan uskup yang ditangkap dekat Jerman pada 1531 menolak makan dan mati tiga hari kemudian.",
        "en": "The Indonesian article also says the bishop-fish caught near Germany in 1531 refused food and died three days later."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c05",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s3",
      "quote": "巨大な魚の姿をしているが、尾鰭が脚、胸鰭が鉤爪のついた腕のように発達しており、頭は円錐形。人間のように直立歩行で移動する。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jepang, ia berwujud ikan besar dengan sirip ekor seperti kaki, sirip dada seperti lengan bercakar, kepala kerucut, dan berjalan tegak seperti manusia.",
        "en": "According to the Japanese article, it looks like a huge fish whose tail fin forms legs and pectoral fins clawed arms, with a conical head, walking upright like a man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c06",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s3",
      "quote": "ビショップ・フィッシュの正体については諸説あり、カスザメ、セイウチ、ズキンアザラシなどの候補が挙げられている。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada berbagai dugaan tentang identitasnya, seperti hiu malaikat, walrus, atau anjing laut bertudung.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c07",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s3",
      "quote": "地上と同じように、海中にも聖職者が存在するキリスト教的な世界観が、ビショップ・フィッシュの創造に影響を与え",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pandangan Kristen bahwa laut pun memiliki rohaniwan seperti di darat ikut membentuk gagasan ikan uskup.",
        "en": "The Christian view that the sea, like the land, has its own clergy shaped the idea of the bishop-fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bishop-fish-c08",
      "source_id": "bishop-fish-s3",
      "quote": "ビショップ・フィッシュ（「海の司教」の意）またはシー・モンク（「海の修道士」の意）とは、中世ヨーロッパの民話に登場する伝説の海洋生物。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jepang menyamakan ikan uskup dengan Sea monk (\"biarawan laut\"), makhluk laut legendaris dalam cerita rakyat Eropa abad pertengahan.",
        "en": "The Japanese article equates the bishop-fish with the Sea monk ('monk of the sea'), a legendary sea creature of medieval European folklore."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Coco atau Cuco adalah momok mirip hantu dari Portugal dan Spanyol yang datang pada malam hari membawa pergi anak-anak yang tidak patuh.",
    "en": "The Coco or Cuco is a ghost-like bogeyman of Portugal and Spain who comes at night to carry off disobedient children.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "coco-folklore-c01",
      "coco-folklore-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Coco, Coca, Cuco, atau Cucuy adalah monster mirip hantu padanan bogeyman yang berasal dari Portugal utara dan Galisia lalu menyebar ke negara-negara berbahasa Spanyol dan Portugis. Ia datang pada malam hari ke rumah anak yang nakal atau tidak mau tidur dan membawa mereka pergi; ia tidak memiliki wujud pasti. Namanya terkait kata coco, \"kepala\" atau \"tengkorak\".",
      "en": "The Coco, Coca, Cuco or Cucuy is a ghost-like bogeyman that began in northern Portugal and Galicia and spread through Spanish- and Portuguese-speaking countries. It comes at night to the homes of children who misbehave or will not sleep and carries them off; it has no fixed form. Its name is tied to coco, 'head' or 'skull'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "coco-folklore-c01",
        "coco-folklore-c02",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Amerika Latin ia disebut El Cuco dan di New Mexico \"the Coco Man\". Di Brasil, Cuca digambarkan sebagai buaya perempuan berambut pirang, wujud yang sebenarnya berasal dari acara televisi tahun 1952, sedangkan Monteiro Lobato dalam O saci (1921) hanya menyebut wajah buaya dan jari bercakar.",
      "en": "Many Latin American countries call it El Cuco and New Mexico 'the Coco Man'.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Coco or Coca (also known as the Cucuy, Cuco, Cuca, Cucu, Cucuí or El Cucuí) is a mythical ghost-like monster, equivalent to the bogeyman, found in Portugal and Spain. Those beliefs have also spread in many Hispanophone and Lusophone countries.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Coco atau Coca (juga Cucuy, Cuco, Cuca, atau El Cucuí) adalah monster mitis mirip hantu, padanan bogeyman, di Portugal dan Spanyol, yang kepercayaannya juga menyebar ke banyak negara berbahasa Spanyol dan Portugis.",
        "en": "The Coco or Coca (also Cucuy, Cuco, Cuca or El Cucuí) is a mythical ghost-like monster, the bogeyman's equivalent, found in Portugal and Spain, whose belief has spread to many Spanish- and Portuguese-speaking countries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Cucuy is a male being while Cuca is a female version of the mythical monster. The \"monster\" will come to the house of disobedient children at night and take them away.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cucuy adalah sosok laki-laki dan Cuca versi perempuannya; monster ini datang pada malam hari ke rumah anak yang tidak patuh dan membawa mereka pergi.",
        "en": "The Cucuy is male and the Cuca the female version; the monster comes at night to the homes of disobedient children and takes them away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The myth of the Coco, or Cucuy, originated in northern Portugal and Galicia. The word coco is used in colloquial speech to refer to the human head in Spanish. Coco also means \"skull\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos Coco atau Cucuy berasal dari Portugal utara dan Galisia; dalam bahasa Spanyol sehari-hari coco berarti kepala manusia, dan juga \"tengkorak\".",
        "en": "The myth of the Coco or Cucuy began in northern Portugal and Galicia; colloquially in Spanish coco means the human head, and also 'skull'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "In Brazilian folklore, the monster is referred to as Cuca and pictured as a female humanoid alligator with blond hair. Though this reptilian form resembles the Portuguese coca, a dragon, it actually originates from the 1952's TV show Sítio do Pica-pau Amarelo (1952 TV series).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Brasil ia disebut Cuca, digambarkan sebagai buaya perempuan mirip manusia berambut pirang; wujud reptil ini sebenarnya berasal dari acara televisi Sítio do Pica-pau Amarelo tahun 1952.",
        "en": "In Brazilian folklore it is the Cuca, pictured as a blonde female humanoid alligator; this reptile form actually comes from the 1952 TV show Sítio do Pica-pau Amarelo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "In the only book she appears, O saci (1921), Cuca's description is vague, Monteiro Lobato only states that she had an \"alligator face\" and \"clawed fingers\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu-satunya buku yang memuatnya, O saci (1921), Monteiro Lobato hanya menyebut Cuca berwajah buaya dan berjari bercakar.",
        "en": "In the only book she appears in, O saci (1921), Monteiro Lobato says only that Cuca had an 'alligator face' and 'clawed fingers'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Many Latin American countries refer to the monster as El Cuco. In northern New Mexico and southern Colorado, where there is a large Hispanic population, it is referred to by its anglicized name, \"the Coco Man\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak negara Amerika Latin menyebutnya El Cuco, dan di New Mexico utara serta Colorado selatan ia disebut \"the Coco Man\".",
        "en": "Many Latin American countries call it El Cuco, and in northern New Mexico and southern Colorado it is 'the Coco Man'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Le Coco, aussi nommé Cuco, Cucuy ou Cuculelé dans les pays d'Amérique latine, est une créature du folklore hispanique et portugais correspondant à une sorte de croque-mitaine qui vient punir les enfants qui se sont mal conduits.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Coco, juga Cuco, Cucuy, atau Cuculelé, adalah semacam momok dalam cerita rakyat Spanyol dan Portugal yang datang menghukum anak nakal.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the Coco, also Cuco, Cucuy or Cuculelé, is a kind of bogeyman of Hispanic and Portuguese folklore who comes to punish badly behaved children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coco-folklore-c08",
      "source_id": "coco-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Es a menudo descrita como un agente disciplinario, cuya función principal es emplear tácticas de intimidación con fines disuasorios para inducir la obediencia en los menores, generalmente bajo la amenaza de \"llevarse\" o aprehender a aquellos que exhiben conductas inapropiadas o presentan resistencia al sueño. Carece de una forma física definida",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, ia sering digambarkan sebagai alat disiplin yang menakuti anak agar patuh, dengan ancaman akan \"membawa pergi\" anak yang nakal atau tidak mau tidur, dan tidak memiliki wujud fisik yang pasti.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, it is often described as a disciplinary agent that frightens children into obedience under threat of 'taking away' those who misbehave or will not sleep, and it has no defined physical form."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ksatria Angsa adalah pahlawan legendaris abad pertengahan yang datang dengan perahu ditarik angsa untuk membela seorang gadis dengan syarat namanya tak boleh ditanyakan, kisah yang kelak menjadi opera Lohengrin.",
    "en": "The Knight of the Swan is a legendary medieval hero who arrives in a swan-drawn boat to defend a damsel on condition his name is never asked, a tale that became the opera Lohengrin.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "knight-of-the-swan-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ksatria Angsa adalah penyelamat misterius yang datang dengan perahu ditarik angsa untuk membela seorang gadis, dengan syarat namanya tidak boleh ditanyakan; setelah mendapat tanah dan istri, ia pergi bersama angsanya. Legenda yang tercatat sejak abad ke-12 ini berasal dari Belanda Burgundia, dan siklus Perang Salib Prancis menjadikannya leluhur Godefroy dari Bouillon.",
      "en": "The Knight of the Swan is a mysterious rescuer who arrives in a swan-drawn boat to defend a damsel on condition that his name is never asked; after winning land and a wife he leaves with his swan. Recorded from the 12th century, the legend comes from the Burgundian Netherlands, and the French Crusade cycle made him an ancestor of Godfrey of Bouillon.",

        "knight-of-the-swan-c01",

        "knight-of-the-swan-c08"

    },
    {
      "id": "Wolfram von Eschenbach menamainya Loherangrin dalam Parzival, Konrad von Würzburg menggubah Der Schwanritter, dan keduanya menjadi dasar opera Lohengrin karya Richard Wagner (1850). Kisah anak-anak angsa yang terkait mirip dongeng The Six Swans.",
      "en": "Wolfram von Eschenbach called him Loherangrin in Parzival, Konrad von Würzburg wrote Der Schwanritter, and both underlie Richard Wagner's opera Lohengrin (1850). The related Swan Children tale resembles The Six Swans.",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-s3",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chevalier_au_cygne",
      "title": "Chevalier au cygne",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c01",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s1",
      "quote": "The story of the Knight of the Swan, or Swan Knight, is a medieval tale about a mysterious rescuer who comes in a swan-drawn boat to defend a damsel, his only condition being that he must never be asked his name.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Ksatria Angsa adalah dongeng abad pertengahan tentang penyelamat misterius yang datang dengan perahu yang ditarik angsa untuk membela seorang gadis, dengan satu-satunya syarat bahwa namanya tidak boleh ditanyakan.",
        "en": "The tale of the Knight of the Swan is a medieval story of a mysterious rescuer who arrives in a swan-drawn boat to defend a damsel, on the sole condition that he must never be asked his name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c02",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest versions, preserved in John of Alta Silva's Dolopathos) do not provide specific identity to this knight, but the Old French Crusade cycle of chansons de geste adapted it to make the Swan Knight (Le Chevalier au Cigne, first version around 1192) the legendary ancestor of Godfrey of Bouillon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi tertua dalam Dolopathos karya John of Alta Silva tidak menyebut identitasnya, tetapi siklus chanson de geste Perang Salib dalam bahasa Prancis Kuno menjadikan Ksatria Angsa (Le Chevalier au Cigne, versi pertama sekitar 1192) leluhur legendaris Godefroy dari Bouillon.",
        "en": "The earliest versions, in John of Alta Silva's Dolopathos, give the knight no identity, but the Old French Crusade cycle of chansons de geste made the Swan Knight (Le Chevalier au Cigne, first version c. 1192) the legendary ancestor of Godfrey of Bouillon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c03",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s1",
      "quote": "At a later time, the German poet Wolfram von Eschenbach incorporated the swan knight Loherangrin into his Matter of Britain epic Parzival (first quarter of the 13th century).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian penyair Jerman Wolfram von Eschenbach memasukkan ksatria angsa Loherangrin ke dalam epos Parzival pada awal abad ke-13.",
        "en": "Later the German poet Wolfram von Eschenbach brought the swan knight Loherangrin into his epic Parzival in the early 13th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c04",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s1",
      "quote": "Wolfram's and Konrad's texts were used to construct the libretto for Richard Wagner's opera Lohengrin (Weimar 1850).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Wolfram dan Konrad von Würzburg menjadi dasar libretto opera Lohengrin karya Richard Wagner (Weimar, 1850).",
        "en": "Wolfram's and Konrad von Würzburg's texts were used for the libretto of Richard Wagner's opera Lohengrin (Weimar, 1850)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c05",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s1",
      "quote": "It also bears resemblance to the fairy tale The Six Swans, where brothers transformed into birds are rescued by the efforts of their sister.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah anak-anak angsa yang terkait legenda ini mirip dongeng The Six Swans, tentang saudara-saudara yang diubah menjadi burung dan diselamatkan saudari mereka.",
        "en": "The related Swan Children tale resembles The Six Swans, in which brothers turned into birds are saved by their sister."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c06",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s2",
      "quote": "Die Schwanenrittersage ist eine in mehreren Varianten überlieferte mittelalterliche Sage aus den Burgundischen Niederlanden. Sie erhielt unterschiedliche Ausformungen in der Literatur (so die Verserzählung „Der Schwanritter“ von Konrad von Würzburg) und diente einigen Herrscherhäusern am Niederrhein als genealogische Ursprungssage.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, legenda Ksatria Angsa berasal dari Belanda Burgundia, digubah antara lain dalam syair Der Schwanritter karya Konrad von Würzburg, dan menjadi legenda asal-usul beberapa wangsa di Rhein Hilir.",
        "en": "According to the German article, the Swan Knight legend comes from the Burgundian Netherlands, was retold in works such as Konrad von Würzburg's verse tale Der Schwanritter, and served several Lower Rhine dynasties as an origin legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c07",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s3",
      "quote": "Dans la version la plus condensée de l’histoire, un inconnu en armes aborde sur un rivage dans une barque remorquée par un cygne. L'inconnu fait preuve de vaillance et obtient en récompense un fief et une épouse, avec qui il a des enfants. Un jour, le cygne réapparaît : l’inconnu saute dans la barque qui est aussitôt entraînée au large par l’oiseau et disparaît comme il était venu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, dalam versi paling ringkas seorang asing bersenjata mendarat dengan perahu yang ditarik angsa, menunjukkan keberanian, mendapat tanah dan istri, lalu suatu hari angsa itu kembali dan ia pergi seperti saat datang.",
        "en": "According to the French article, in the shortest version an armed stranger lands in a swan-towed boat, proves his valour, wins a fief and a wife, and one day, when the swan returns, leaps into the boat and vanishes as he came."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knight-of-the-swan-c08",
      "source_id": "knight-of-the-swan-s3",
      "quote": "Le chevalier au cygne est un personnage légendaire médiéval de l'Europe occidentale, attesté dès le XIIe siècle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ksatria Angsa adalah tokoh legendaris abad pertengahan Eropa Barat yang tercatat sejak abad ke-12.",
        "en": "The Swan Knight is a legendary medieval figure of Western Europe attested from the 12th century."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Yale adalah binatang mitis mirip antelop bertaring babi hutan dengan tanduk yang dapat diputar ke segala arah, dari Plinius Tua dan heraldik Eropa.",
    "en": "The yale is a mythical antelope-like beast with boar's tusks and horns that swivel in any direction, from Pliny the Elder and European heraldry.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "yale-c01",
      "yale-c03",
      "yale-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yale atau centicore adalah binatang mitis mirip antelop atau kambing bertaring babi hutan, yang tanduk besarnya dapat diputar ke segala arah: satu dijulurkan untuk bertarung, yang lain disimpan sebagai cadangan. Plinius Tua menggambarkan eale dari Aethiopia sebesar kuda nil dan berekor gajah, mungkin dari antelop atau kerbau Afrika. Namanya mungkin dari kata Ibrani yael, \"ibex\".",
      "en": "The yale or centicore is a mythical antelope- or goat-like beast with boar's tusks whose great horns swivel in any direction: one forward to fight, the other held in reserve. Pliny the Elder described the eale of Aethiopia as hippo-sized with an elephant's tail, perhaps from African antelope or buffalo. Its name may come from the Hebrew yael, 'ibex'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yale-c01",
        "yale-c02",
        "yale-c03",
        "yale-c04",
        "yale-c05",
        "yale-c08"

    {
      "id": "Dalam bestiari abad pertengahan basilisk adalah musuhnya, dan dalam heraldik ia melambangkan pertahanan yang kuat; yale menjadi binatang heraldik Wangsa Beaufort dan keluarga kerajaan Britania sejak Henry VII sekitar 1485.",
      "en": "In medieval bestiaries the basilisk is its enemy, and in heraldry it stands for strong defence; the yale became a heraldic beast of the House of Beaufort and the British royal family from Henry VII around 1485.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "yale-c01",
      "source_id": "yale-s1",
      "quote": "The yale or centicore (Latin: eale) is a mythical beast found in European mythology and heraldry.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yale atau centicore (Latin: eale) adalah binatang mitis dalam mitologi dan heraldik Eropa.",
        "en": "The yale or centicore (Latin eale) is a mythical beast of European mythology and heraldry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c02",
      "source_id": "yale-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"yale\" is believed to be derived from the Hebrew word יָעֵל (yael), meaning \"ibex\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama yale diyakini berasal dari kata Ibrani yael, \"ibex\".",
        "en": "The name yale is thought to come from the Hebrew yael, 'ibex'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c03",
      "source_id": "yale-s1",
      "quote": "The yale is described as an antelope- or goat-like creature with the tusks of a boar and large horns. These horns possess the ability to swivel in any direction which makes them good for both offensive and defensive attacks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yale digambarkan mirip antelop atau kambing, bertaring babi hutan dan bertanduk besar yang dapat diputar ke segala arah sehingga berguna untuk menyerang maupun bertahan.",
        "en": "The yale is described as antelope- or goat-like, with a boar's tusks and large horns that swivel in any direction, useful for attack and defence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c04",
      "source_id": "yale-s1",
      "quote": "it is the size of the river-horse, has the tail of the elephant, and is of a black or tawny colour. It has also the jaws of the wild boar, and horns that are moveable, and more than a cubit in length",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Plinius Tua dalam Naturalis Historia menggambarkan eale dari Aethiopia sebesar kuda nil, berekor gajah, berwarna hitam atau cokelat kekuningan, berahang babi hutan, dan bertanduk yang dapat digerakkan, lebih panjang dari satu hasta.",
        "en": "Pliny the Elder in his Natural History describes the eale of Aethiopia as the size of a hippopotamus, with an elephant's tail, black or tawny, with a boar's jaws and movable horns over a cubit long."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c05",
      "source_id": "yale-s1",
      "quote": "Pliny reports sighting the yale while on the sub-Saharan African plains. The antelope and buffalo inhabit this region and loosely match his description, so may have given rise to his misunderstanding.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Antelop dan kerbau di dataran Afrika sub-Sahara agak cocok dengan gambaran Plinius dan mungkin menjadi asal salah pahamnya.",
        "en": "Antelope and buffalo of the sub-Saharan plains loosely match Pliny's description and may have caused his misunderstanding."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c06",
      "source_id": "yale-s2",
      "quote": "Das Yale ist unter den heraldischen Bestien der britischen Königsfamilie vertreten. Es fand als „Supporter“ der Streitkräfte von John of Lancaster, Herzog von Bedford, und im englischen House of Beaufort Verwendung. Seine Verbindung mit der britischen Monarchie begann etwa 1485 mit Heinrich VII. Tudor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, yale termasuk binatang heraldik keluarga kerajaan Britania, dipakai sebagai penyangga lambang John of Lancaster, Duke of Bedford, dan oleh Wangsa Beaufort, dan hubungannya dengan monarki Britania bermula sekitar 1485 dengan Henry VII Tudor.",
        "en": "According to the German article, the yale is among the heraldic beasts of the British royal family, used as a supporter by John of Lancaster, Duke of Bedford, and by the House of Beaufort, its link with the British monarchy beginning around 1485 with Henry VII Tudor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c07",
      "source_id": "yale-s2",
      "quote": "Die Kreatur kommt in mittelalterlichen Bestiarien und in der Heraldik vor, wo sie für eine starke Verteidigung steht.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul dalam bestiari abad pertengahan dan heraldik sebagai lambang pertahanan yang kuat.",
        "en": "It appears in medieval bestiaries and heraldry, where it stands for strong defence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c08",
      "source_id": "yale-s3",
      "quote": "戦いの時は、片方の角を前に伸ばして戦い、もう片方の角は後ろに向けておくという。それで、戦っている方の角が傷付くと、もう片方の角と入れ替えるという。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jepang, saat bertarung ia menjulurkan satu tanduk ke depan dan menarik yang lain ke belakang, lalu menukarnya bila tanduk pertama rusak.",
        "en": "According to the Japanese article, in a fight it points one horn forward and keeps the other back, swapping them when the first is damaged."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yale-c09",
      "source_id": "yale-s3",
      "quote": "中世ヨーロッパの動物寓意譚によるとバジリスクはエアレーの敵で、眠っているエアレーを見つけると目を刺し、張り裂けるまで膨らませるという。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut bestiari Eropa abad pertengahan, basilisk adalah musuh yale yang menusuk mata yale yang sedang tidur.",
        "en": "Per medieval European bestiaries, the basilisk is the yale's enemy and pierces the eyes of a sleeping yale."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Churel atau chudail adalah hantu perempuan Asia Selatan berkaki terbalik, arwah perempuan yang mati secara kejam, yang menyamar sebagai perempuan cantik untuk memikat dan membunuh lelaki.",
    "en": "The churel or chudail is a backward-footed female ghost of South Asia, the spirit of a woman who died cruelly, who disguises herself as a beauty to lure and kill men.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "churel-c01",
      "churel-c02",
      "churel-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Churel, chudail, atau petni adalah hantu perempuan dari Asia Selatan, Asia Tenggara, dan Karibia, \"hantu makhluk hidup yang tidak disucikan\" yang sering menempel di pohon. Perempuan yang mati secara kejam, misalnya saat melahirkan atau karena siksaan mertua, kembali sebagai churel untuk membalas dendam pada lelaki keluarganya. Ia buruk rupa dengan kaki menghadap ke belakang, tetapi menyamar sebagai perempuan cantik untuk memikat lelaki ke hutan dan mengisap daya hidupnya.",
      "en": "The churel, chudail or petni is a female ghost of South Asia, Southeast Asia and the Caribbean, 'the ghost of an unpurified living thing' that clings to trees. A woman who dies cruelly, for example in childbirth or through her in-laws' abuse, returns as a churel to take revenge on her family's men. She is hideous with backward feet but takes the form of a beautiful woman to lure men into the forest and drain their life.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "churel-c01",
        "churel-c02",
        "churel-c03",
        "churel-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Keluarga perempuan yang mati tragis melakukan ritual dan mengubur jasadnya dengan cara khusus agar ia tidak kembali. Ia dikenal sebagai Pichal Peri di Punjab dan Pontianak di Malaysia dan Indonesia, dan legendanya konon berasal dari Persia.",
      "en": "Families of women who die tragically perform rites and bury the body in special ways to stop her return. She is known as Pichal Peri in Punjab and Pontianak in Malaysia and Indonesia, and the legend supposedly came from Persia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "churel-c04",
        "churel-c05",
        "churel-c06",
        "churel-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "churel-c01",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "is a mythical or legendary creature resembling a woman, which may be a demonical revenant said to occur in South Asia, Southeast Asia, and the Caribbean, particularly popular in India, Bangladesh, Nepal, Pakistan, Trinidad and Tobago, Guyana, and Suriname.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Churel adalah makhluk mitis berwujud perempuan, mungkin mayat hidup yang jahat, yang dikenal di Asia Selatan, Asia Tenggara, dan Karibia, terutama di India, Bangladesh, Nepal, Pakistan, Trinidad dan Tobago, Guyana, dan Suriname.",
        "en": "The churel is a mythical woman-like creature, perhaps a demonic revenant, found in South Asia, Southeast Asia and the Caribbean, especially India, Bangladesh, Nepal, Pakistan, Trinidad and Tobago, Guyana and Suriname."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c02",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "The churel is typically described as  \"the ghost of an unpurified living thing\", but because she is often said to latch on to trees, she is also called a tree-spirit. According to some legends, a woman who dies very cruelly will come back as a revenant churel for revenge, particularly targeting the males in her family.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Churel biasa disebut \"hantu makhluk hidup yang tidak disucikan\", juga roh pohon karena sering menempel di pohon; menurut sebagian legenda perempuan yang mati secara kejam kembali sebagai churel untuk membalas dendam, terutama kepada lelaki dalam keluarganya.",
        "en": "The churel is usually called 'the ghost of an unpurified living thing', also a tree-spirit since she clings to trees; by some legends a woman who dies cruelly returns as a churel for revenge, especially on the men of her family."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c03",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "The churel is mostly described as extremely ugly and hideous but is able to shape-shift and disguise herself as a beautiful woman to lure men into the woods or mountains where she either kills them or sucks up their life-force or virility, turning them into old men. Their feet are believed to be turned the other way around, so the toes face the direction of their back.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Churel biasanya sangat buruk rupa, tetapi dapat menyamar sebagai perempuan cantik untuk memikat lelaki ke hutan atau gunung, lalu membunuh mereka atau mengisap daya hidupnya hingga menjadi tua; kakinya menghadap ke belakang.",
        "en": "The churel is usually hideous but can disguise herself as a beautiful woman to lure men into the woods or hills, where she kills them or drains their life force until they turn old; her feet point backwards."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c04",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "The family of a woman who dies a traumatic, tragic, or unnatural death might perform special rituals fearing that the victimised woman might return as a churel. The corpses of suspected churels are also buried in a particular method and posture so as to prevent her from returning.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keluarga perempuan yang mati secara tragis atau tidak wajar dapat menggelar ritual khusus agar ia tidak kembali sebagai churel, dan jasad yang dicurigai dikubur dengan cara dan posisi tertentu.",
        "en": "The family of a woman who dies a tragic or unnatural death may hold special rites lest she return as a churel, and suspected corpses are buried in a particular way and posture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c05",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "The churel is known as the Pichal Peri in the Punjab region of India and Pakistan, Petni/Shakchunni in the Bengal region, and Pontianak in Malaysia and Indonesia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Churel dikenal sebagai Pichal Peri di Punjab, Petni atau Shakchunni di Bengal, dan Pontianak di Malaysia dan Indonesia.",
        "en": "The churel is known as Pichal Peri in Punjab, Petni or Shakchunni in Bengal, and Pontianak in Malaysia and Indonesia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c06",
      "source_id": "churel-s1",
      "quote": "The legend of Churel supposedly originated from Persia where they were described as being the spirits of women who died with \"grossly unsatisfied desires\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda churel konon berasal dari Persia, tempat mereka digambarkan sebagai roh perempuan yang mati dengan hasrat yang sangat tidak terpenuhi.",
        "en": "The churel legend supposedly began in Persia, where they were the spirits of women who died with 'grossly unsatisfied desires'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c07",
      "source_id": "churel-s2",
      "quote": "Esistono molti rimedi popolari e detti che spiegano come liberarsi dalle Churel e diverse misure per impedire il loro ritorno in vita.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, ada banyak penangkal rakyat dan pepatah tentang cara mengusir churel dan mencegahnya kembali.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, many folk remedies and sayings explain how to get rid of churels and stop them returning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "churel-c08",
      "source_id": "churel-s3",
      "quote": "কিছুমান কিংবদন্তি অনুসৰি প্ৰসৱ বা গৰ্ভাৱস্থাৰ সময়ত বা শহুৰেকৰ হাতত দুখ-কষ্টৰ ফলত মৃত্যু হোৱা মহিলাগৰাকীয়ে প্ৰতিশোধৰ বাবে, বিশেষকৈ পৰিয়ালৰ পুৰুষসকলক লক্ষ্য কৰি প্ৰেতনী হিচাপে ঘূৰি আহে।",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Assam, perempuan yang mati saat melahirkan, saat hamil, atau karena siksaan mertuanya kembali sebagai churel untuk membalas dendam, terutama kepada lelaki keluarganya.",
        "en": "According to the Assamese article, a woman who dies in childbirth, in pregnancy or through her in-laws' cruelty returns as a churel for revenge, especially on the family's men."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Sigmund adalah pahlawan mitologi Jermanik dalam saga Völsunga, putra Völsung dan ayah Sigurð si pembunuh naga. Ia satu-satunya yang mampu mencabut pedang Gram yang ditancapkan Odin ke pohon Barnstokk.       "en": "Sigmund is a hero of Germanic myth in the Völsunga saga, son of Völsung and father of Sigurð the dragon-slayer.       "en": "With his son Sinfjötli, born of his sister Signý, he lived as an outlaw, for a time as a werewolf, and then killed Siggeir. He is also mentioned in Beowulf and his story is adapted in Richard Wagner's opera Die Walküre.",

    {
      "id": "sigmund-c01",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s1",
      "quote": "In Germanic mythology, Sigmund (Old Norse: Sigmundr [ˈsiɣˌmundz̠], Old English: Sigemund) is a hero whose story is told in the Völsunga saga.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Jermanik, Sigmund (Norse Kuno: Sigmundr; Inggris Kuno: Sigemund) adalah pahlawan yang kisahnya diceritakan dalam saga Völsunga.",
        "en": "In Germanic mythology Sigmund (Old Norse Sigmundr, Old English Sigemund) is a hero whose story is told in the Völsunga saga."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c02",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s1",
      "quote": "He and his sister, Signý, are the children of Völsung and his wife Hljod. Sigmund is best known as the father of Sigurð the dragon-slayer",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dan saudarinya Signý adalah anak Völsung dan istrinya Hljod, dan Sigmund paling dikenal sebagai ayah Sigurð si pembunuh naga.",
        "en": "He and his sister Signý are the children of Völsung and his wife Hljod, and Sigmund is best known as the father of Sigurð the dragon-slayer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c03",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s1",
      "quote": "when Odin, disguised as a beggar, plunges a sword (Gram) into the living tree Barnstokk (\"offspring-trunk\") around which Völsung's hall is built. The disguised Odin announces that the man who can remove the sword will have it as a gift. Only Sigmund is able to free the sword from the tree.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Odin yang menyamar sebagai pengemis menancapkan pedang Gram ke pohon Barnstokk di tengah balairung Völsung dan menyatakan siapa pun yang mencabutnya boleh memilikinya; hanya Sigmund yang berhasil.",
        "en": "Odin, disguised as a beggar, thrusts the sword Gram into the tree Barnstokk in Völsung's hall, saying whoever pulls it out may keep it; only Sigmund succeeds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c04",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s1",
      "quote": "When the Völsung clan arrive, they are attacked by the Gauts; King Völsung is killed and his sons captured.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika klan Völsung datang ke Gautland, mereka diserang orang Gaut; Raja Völsung tewas dan putra-putranya ditawan.",
        "en": "When the Völsungs arrive in Gautland they are attacked by the Gauts; King Völsung is killed and his sons captured."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c05",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s1",
      "quote": "On the ninth night, she has a servant smear honey on Sigmund's face and when the she-wolf arrives, she starts licking the honey off and sticks her tongue into Sigmund's mouth, whereupon Sigmund bites her tongue off, killing her.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah serigala betina jelmaan ibu Siggeir memangsa saudara-saudaranya satu per satu, pada malam kesembilan Signý menyuruh pelayan mengoleskan madu ke wajah Sigmund; ketika serigala itu menjilat dan memasukkan lidahnya ke mulut Sigmund, ia menggigit putus lidahnya.",
        "en": "After the she-wolf, Siggeir's shapeshifting mother, devours his brothers one by one, on the ninth night Signý has honey smeared on Sigmund's face; when the wolf licks it and puts her tongue in his mouth, he bites it off and kills her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c06",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s2",
      "quote": "Mit seiner Zwillingsschwester Signy zeugte er einen Sohn Sinfiötli, mit dem zusammen er den Mord an seinem Vater Wölsung rächte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, bersama saudari kembarnya Signy ia memperoleh putra Sinfjötli, yang bersamanya membalas pembunuhan Völsung.",
        "en": "According to the German article, with his twin sister Signy he fathered Sinfjötli, with whom he avenged Völsung's murder."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c07",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s2",
      "quote": "Die beiden lebten als Geächtete, erlebten einige Abenteuer, zwischenzeitlich auch als Werwölfe, bis es ihnen gelang ihren Vater/Großvater Wölsung zu rächen, indem sie Siggeir töteten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keduanya hidup sebagai buronan, sempat menjadi manusia serigala, sampai berhasil membalas dendam dengan membunuh Siggeir.",
        "en": "The two lived as outlaws, for a time as werewolves, until they avenged Völsung by killing Siggeir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sigmund-c08",
      "source_id": "sigmund-s3",
      "quote": "Il apparaît notamment dans la Völsunga saga, la Þiðrekssaga, et est également mentionné dans le poème anglo-saxon Beowulf sous le nom de Sigemund. Une adaptation de son histoire apparaît dans l'opéra La Walkyrie (Die Walküre) du cycle de L'Anneau du Nibelung (Der Ring des Nibelungen), de Richard Wagner.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, ia muncul dalam saga Völsunga dan Þiðrekssaga, disebut sebagai Sigemund dalam Beowulf, dan kisahnya diadaptasi dalam opera Die Walküre dari siklus Der Ring des Nibelungen karya Richard Wagner.",
        "en": "According to the French article, he appears in the Völsunga saga and Þiðrekssaga, is named Sigemund in Beowulf, and his story is adapted in the opera Die Walküre of Richard Wagner's Ring cycle."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aatxe, \"banteng muda\", adalah roh gua Basque berwujud banteng merah yang keluar pada malam badai untuk menghukum penjahat, sebagai wakil dewi Mari.",
    "en": "Aatxe, 'young bull', is a Basque cave spirit in the form of a red bull that comes out on stormy nights to punish criminals, as the goddess Mari's agent.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "aatxe-c01",
      "aatxe-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aatxe atau Aatxegorri, \"banteng muda (merah)\", adalah roh gua Basque yang berwujud banteng merah tetapi dapat menjadi manusia, sapi, atau anak sapi. Pada malam badai ia keluar dari sarangnya, menyerang penjahat, dan menurut artikel bahasa Jerman menggiring penduduk ke dalam rumah. Ia diduga wakil atau pelaksana kehendak dewi Mari, dan kadang disebut Etsai.",
      "en": "Aatxe or Aatxegorri, 'young (red) bull', is a Basque cave spirit in the form of a red bull who can become a man, cow or calf. On stormy nights he leaves his lair and attacks criminals, and per the German article drives people indoors. He is thought to be the goddess Mari's agent or enforcer, and is sometimes called Etsai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aatxe-c01",
        "aatxe-c02",
        "aatxe-c03",
        "aatxe-c04",
        "aatxe-c06"

    {
      "id": "Gua-gua yang dikaitkan dengannya, seperti Isturits dan Sare, memuat lukisan banteng Paleolitik, sehingga mitos ini mungkin sangat tua. Artikel bahasa Prancis menyebut Aatxe anak Behigorri, sapi merah.",
      "en": "Caves linked with him, such as Isturits and Sare, hold Palaeolithic bull paintings, so the myth may be very old. The French article calls Aatxe the young of Behigorri, the red cow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aatxe-c05",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c01",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s1",
      "quote": "Aatxe (pronounced [a.atʃe]; a contraction of aratxe) is a spirit in the folk mythology of the Basque people. His name is literally translated as \"Young Bull\", and he is sometimes known as Etsai.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aatxe (kependekan dari aratxe) adalah roh dalam mitologi rakyat Basque; namanya berarti \"banteng muda\", dan kadang ia disebut Etsai.",
        "en": "Aatxe (a contraction of aratxe) is a spirit of Basque folk mythology; his name means 'young bull', and he is sometimes called Etsai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c02",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s1",
      "quote": "He is a cave-dwelling spirit who adopts the form of a young red bull, but being a shapeshifter, sometimes takes the shape of a man. Aatxe is also described as a shapeshifter that may appear not only as a bull but also as a cow or calf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia roh penghuni gua yang berwujud banteng muda merah, tetapi sebagai pengubah wujud kadang menjadi manusia, sapi, atau anak sapi.",
        "en": "He is a cave-dwelling spirit in the form of a young red bull, but as a shapeshifter sometimes becomes a man, a cow or a calf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c03",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s1",
      "quote": "At night, more so in stormy weather, he arises from the hollow which is his lair. He attacks criminals and other malevolent people.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam hari, terutama saat badai, ia keluar dari lubang sarangnya dan menyerang penjahat serta orang jahat lain.",
        "en": "At night, especially in storms, he comes out of his lair and attacks criminals and other wicked people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c04",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s1",
      "quote": "He is theorized to be a representative of the goddess Mari, or may be an enforcer of her will, punishing people who cheat her. Another name for him is Aatxegorri which means \"young red bull\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diduga wakil dewi Mari atau pelaksana kehendaknya yang menghukum orang yang menipunya; nama lainnya Aatxegorri, \"banteng muda merah\".",
        "en": "He is thought to represent the goddess Mari or enforce her will, punishing those who cheat her; another name is Aatxegorri, 'young red bull'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c05",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed Aatxe inhabited caves and hollows; in many (Isturits, Sare, Errenteria, among others) engravings and paintings depicting aurochs, bulls, and oxen have been found; which implies that this Basque myth has its origins in the Paleolithic.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di banyak gua yang dihuni Aatxe, seperti Isturits, Sare, dan Errenteria, ditemukan ukiran dan lukisan aurochs, banteng, dan lembu, yang menunjukkan mitos ini berakar pada zaman Paleolitik.",
        "en": "In many caves Aatxe was thought to inhabit, such as Isturits, Sare and Errenteria, engravings and paintings of aurochs, bulls and oxen have been found, implying the myth goes back to the Palaeolithic."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c06",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s2",
      "quote": "Die Aatxe oder Aataxe sind Höhlengeister aus der Mythologie des Baskenlandes, die in stürmischen Nächten ihre Höhle Leze (bei Sara) verlassen und die Bewohner der Dörfer und Städte in die Häuser treiben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, Aatxe adalah roh gua yang pada malam badai keluar dari gua Leze dekat Sara dan menggiring penduduk desa masuk ke rumah.",
        "en": "According to the German article, the Aatxe are cave spirits that on stormy nights leave the Leze cave near Sare and drive villagers into their houses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aatxe-c07",
      "source_id": "aatxe-s3",
      "quote": "Son petit, Aatxe qui signifie veau en basque est aussi appelé Behigorri, Aatxegorri ou Aatexegorrila, Txaalgorri, Txalgorri ou Txahalgorri.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis tentang Behigorri, Aatxe (\"anak sapi\") adalah anak Behigorri dan juga disebut Aatxegorri atau Txahalgorri.",
        "en": "According to the French article on Behigorri, Aatxe ('calf') is Behigorri's young, also called Aatxegorri or Txahalgorri."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Basajaun, \"Tuan hutan\", adalah makhluk liar raksasa berbulu dalam mitologi Basque yang tinggal di hutan pegunungan, melindungi kawanan ternak, dan mengajari manusia bertani dan menempa besi.",
    "en": "Basajaun, 'Lord of the forest', is a giant hairy wild being of Basque mythology who lives in mountain forests, guards the flocks and taught humans farming and ironworking.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "basajaun-c01",
      "basajaun-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Basajaun atau Baxajaun, \"Tuan hutan\", adalah makhluk mirip manusia yang tinggi dan berbulu sampai lutut, berjalan tegak, dengan satu kaki normal dan satu kaki bulat seperti tunggul pohon yang meninggalkan jejak melingkar; kadang ia bermata satu seperti Tartalo. Ia tinggal di hutan dan gua dataran tinggi seperti Gorbea, Ataun, dan Irati, melindungi kawanan ternak, dan mengajari manusia bertani dan menempa besi.",
      "en": "Basajaun or Baxajaun, 'Lord of the forest', is a tall humanlike being with knee-length hair who walks upright, one foot normal and the other round like a stump, leaving circular tracks; sometimes he has one eye like the Tartalo. He lives in highland forests and caves such as Gorbea, Ataun and Irati, guards the flocks and taught humans farming and smithing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "basajaun-c01",
        "basajaun-c03",
        "basajaun-c04",
        "basajaun-c05",
        "basajaun-c06",
        "basajaun-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia kadang masuk ke rumah atau kandang mengambil makanan tanpa ada yang berani menghalangi, sehingga ditakuti dan dihormati. Ia mirip \"lelaki liar\" Eropa dan Silvanus. Di Aragon ia disebut Basajun atau Bonjarau, dan pasangan perempuannya Basandere mungkin diciptakan belakangan.",
      "en": "He sometimes enters houses or folds to take food without anyone daring to stop him, so he is feared and respected. He resembles the 'wild men' of European traditions and Silvanus. In Aragon he is Basajun or Bonjarau, and his female counterpart Basandere was probably invented later.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "basajaun-c02",
        "basajaun-c07",
        "basajaun-c08",
        "basajaun-c09"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c01",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "is a huge, hairy hominid dwelling in the woods. They were thought to protect flocks of livestock and teach skills such as agriculture and ironworking to humans.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Basque, Basajaun (\"Tuan hutan\", jamak basajaunak) adalah makhluk mirip manusia yang besar dan berbulu, tinggal di hutan, yang dipercaya melindungi kawanan ternak dan mengajari manusia bertani dan menempa besi.",
        "en": "In Basque mythology Basajaun ('Lord of the forest', plural basajaunak) is a huge hairy hominid living in the woods, thought to protect flocks and teach humans farming and ironworking."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c02",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "Basajaun (var. Baxajaun, Basque pronunciation: [baʃajaun]) is glossed \"Lord of the forest\" or the \"wild lord\". The female counterpart is the Basandere (var. Basa Andre, Basa-Andre,) probably created during a later period by analogy.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Basajaun (juga Baxajaun) berarti \"Tuan hutan\" atau \"tuan liar\", dan pasangan perempuannya Basandere mungkin diciptakan belakangan berdasarkan analogi.",
        "en": "Basajaun (also Baxajaun) means 'Lord of the forest' or 'wild lord', and his female counterpart, the Basandere, was probably created later by analogy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c03",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "The Basajaun is said to have a tall, human form with his face and body covered in hair, with the strands reaching the knees, walking upright like a man.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Basajaun berwujud manusia yang tinggi, wajah dan tubuhnya tertutup rambut sampai lutut, dan berjalan tegak seperti manusia.",
        "en": "The Basajaun is tall and humanlike, his face and body covered in hair reaching the knees, walking upright like a man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c04",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "His two feet are differently shaped, thus leaving an odd set of foot tracks. While one foot is normal, the other one (left foot) is rounded like the tree stump (base of a tree trunk) and leaves circular footprints.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua kakinya berbeda bentuk: satu normal, sedangkan kaki kirinya bulat seperti tunggul pohon dan meninggalkan jejak melingkar.",
        "en": "His two feet differ: one is normal, while the left is round like a tree stump and leaves circular tracks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c05",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "He may have a single eye in the middle of the forehead, though this is also the attribute of the Tartalo with which the Basajaun is often confounded.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kadang bermata satu di tengah dahi, ciri yang juga dimiliki Tartalo, raksasa yang sering dicampuradukkan dengannya.",
        "en": "He may have a single eye in the middle of his forehead, also a trait of the Tartalo, with whom he is often confused."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c06",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "The basajaunak dwell in the forests and caverns at higher altitudes or \"prominent places\". More specifically, it is said to inhabit the mountainside of the Gorbea in Biscay Province (or the forests of Zeanuri town nearby), around Ataun town which is the backwoods of neighboring Gipuzkoa Province, and the Irati Forest of the Pyrenees",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Basajaunak tinggal di hutan dan gua di dataran tinggi, misalnya lereng Gorbea di Biscaya, sekitar Ataun di Gipuzkoa, dan Hutan Irati di Pyrenees.",
        "en": "The basajaunak live in forests and caves at higher altitudes, such as the slopes of Gorbea in Biscay, around Ataun in Gipuzkoa and the Irati Forest in the Pyrenees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c07",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s1",
      "quote": "The creature is called Basajun in the Basque-Navarre valleys as well as the Aragonese Pyrenees to where the name may have been transmitted (from Basque country) while the creature is called Basajarau, Bonjarau in the Tena Valley and the vales of the cities of Anso and Broto in Aragon",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut Basajun di lembah Basque-Navarra dan Pyrenees Aragon, serta Basajarau atau Bonjarau di lembah Tena, Ansó, dan Broto di Aragon.",
        "en": "It is called Basajun in the Basque-Navarre valleys and Aragonese Pyrenees, and Basajarau or Bonjarau in the Tena, Ansó and Broto valleys of Aragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c08",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s2",
      "quote": "Il pénètre parfois dans les maisons ou les bergeries, se sert sans vergogne de la nourriture préparée, sans que les humains n'osent intervenir. Il inspire crainte et respect.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, ia kadang masuk ke rumah atau kandang domba dan mengambil makanan yang sudah disiapkan tanpa ada yang berani menghalangi, sehingga ia ditakuti sekaligus dihormati.",
        "en": "According to the French article he sometimes enters houses or sheepfolds and helps himself to prepared food without anyone daring to stop him, inspiring fear and respect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c09",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s2",
      "quote": "Par ses caractères physiques et son comportement, il s'apparente aux « hommes sauvages » de nombreuses traditions européennes ou aux sylvains (Sylvanus), protecteurs antiques de la nature et des activités agro-pastorales",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciri dan perilakunya mirip \"lelaki liar\" dalam banyak tradisi Eropa atau Silvanus, pelindung kuno alam serta pertanian dan penggembalaan.",
        "en": "In body and behaviour he resembles the 'wild men' of many European traditions or Silvanus, ancient protector of nature and of farming and herding."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basajaun-c10",
      "source_id": "basajaun-s3",
      "quote": "バシャジャウンは、畜産羊の群れを守り、人間に農業や鉄鍛冶の技術を教えた、とされている。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jepang juga menyebut Basajaun melindungi kawanan domba dan mengajari manusia bertani dan menempa besi.",
        "en": "The Japanese article also says Basajaun guarded the flocks and taught humans farming and smithing."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan ja; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "claims",

    },
    {
      "field": "images",

    }
  ]
}
