      "id": "peluda-s3",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%9A%E3%83%AB%E3%83%BC%E3%83%80",
      "title": "ペルーダ",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ja)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "peluda-c01",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "The Peluda in Spanish, or La Velue ('The Hairy One') originally in French, is a mythical beast that terrorized the environs of the River Huisne, France, during the Middle Ages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peluda dalam bahasa Spanyol, atau La Velue (\"Yang Berbulu\") dalam bahasa Prancis aslinya, adalah binatang mitis yang meneror sekitar Sungai Huisne, Prancis, pada Abad Pertengahan.",
        "en": "The Peluda (Spanish) or La Velue ('The Hairy One', originally French) is a mythical beast that terrorised the River Huisne area of France in the Middle Ages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c02",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "The supposed serpent-headed creature had a body covered in long green fur with poison-tipped spines protruding. It caused floods (or shot out fire from its mouth) that destroyed crops, devoured livestock and humans, and struck humans and animals dead with its tail.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk berkepala ular ini bertubuh tertutup bulu hijau panjang dengan duri berujung racun; ia menimbulkan banjir (atau menyemburkan api) yang merusak panen, memakan ternak dan manusia, dan membunuh dengan ekornya.",
        "en": "This serpent-headed creature had a body of long green fur with poison-tipped spines; it caused floods (or spat fire) that ruined crops, ate livestock and people, and killed with its tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c03",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "It was defeated after it tried to prey upon a maiden named l'Agnelle; her fiancé slew it by delivering a sword-strike to the tail, its only weak spot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikalahkan setelah mencoba memangsa gadis bernama l'Agnelle; tunangannya membunuhnya dengan menebas ekornya, satu-satunya titik lemahnya.",
        "en": "It was defeated after trying to prey on a maiden named l'Agnelle; her fiancé killed it with a sword-stroke to the tail, its only weak spot."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c04",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "The velue  (French, meaning ‘shaggy/hairy one’) was introduced as \"la Peluda\", in Spanish, in Jorge Luis Borges's Book of Imaginary Beings (1957)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Velue, \"yang berbulu\" dalam bahasa Prancis, diperkenalkan sebagai \"la Peluda\" dalam bahasa Spanyol dalam Buku Makhluk Khayalan karya Jorge Luis Borges (1957).",
        "en": "The velue, French for 'shaggy one', was introduced as 'la Peluda' in Spanish in Jorge Luis Borges's Book of Imaginary Beings (1957)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c05",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "A pamphlet of \"La Velue\" was printed in 1889 for the Sarthe area, and its text also gave a physical description of the mythical beast in similar language.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah pamflet tentang \"La Velue\" dicetak pada 1889 untuk daerah Sarthe, dan teksnya juga memuat gambaran fisik binatang itu.",
        "en": "A pamphlet on 'La Velue' was printed in 1889 for the Sarthe area, its text also describing the beast."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c06",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "The French sources tell that the beast was serpent-headed and serpent-tailed, ox-sized with an egg-shaped body covered in \"long green fur\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber Prancis menyebut binatang ini berkepala dan berekor ular, sebesar lembu, dengan tubuh bulat telur yang tertutup \"bulu hijau panjang\".",
        "en": "French sources say the beast was serpent-headed and serpent-tailed, ox-sized, with an egg-shaped body covered in 'long green fur'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c07",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "It was the opinion of Cordonnier-Détrie that the beast belonged to the same family as the mythical Tarasque of Tarascon and Beaucaire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Cordonnier-Détrie, binatang ini sekeluarga dengan Tarasque dari Tarascon dan Beaucaire.",
        "en": "Cordonnier-Détrie held that the beast belonged to the same family as the Tarasque of Tarascon and Beaucaire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c08",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "According to the lore, the beast was excluded from Noah's Ark, yet survived the Deluge, i.e., the Biblical Flood.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita, binatang ini tidak diterima di Bahtera Nuh tetapi tetap selamat dari Air Bah.",
        "en": "According to the lore, the beast was refused a place on Noah's Ark yet survived the Flood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c09",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "According to popular tradition, it died at the bridge of Yvré-l'Évêque. The victory was long celebrated afterwards at La Ferté-Bernard and Connerré, and the people stuffed (or embalmed) the body of the beast, so it has been told.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tradisi, ia mati di jembatan Yvré-l'Évêque; kemenangan itu lama dirayakan di La Ferté-Bernard dan Connerré, dan konon bangkainya diawetkan.",
        "en": "By popular tradition it died at the bridge of Yvré-l'Évêque; the victory was long celebrated at La Ferté-Bernard and Connerré, and its body was reportedly stuffed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c10",
      "source_id": "peluda-s1",
      "quote": "Also at Tuffé, a velue fountain has been installed in 2007, on the open square facing the Église Saint-Pierre-et-Saint-Paul.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Tuffé, sebuah air mancur berbentuk velue dipasang pada 2007 di alun-alun depan Gereja Saint-Pierre-et-Saint-Paul.",
        "en": "At Tuffé a velue fountain was installed in 2007 in the square facing the Church of Saint-Pierre-et-Saint-Paul."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c11",
      "source_id": "peluda-s2",
      "quote": "Meskipun makhluk ini merupakan makhluk dalam legenda Prancis, namanya berasal dari bahasa Occitan yang berarti \"berambut\". Tubuhnya berbentuk seperti landak dan besarnya seperti seekor lembu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Indonesia menyebut namanya berasal dari bahasa Occitan yang berarti \"berambut\", tubuhnya seperti landak, dan besarnya seperti lembu.",
        "en": "The Indonesian article says its name comes from Occitan for 'hairy', its body is like a porcupine's, and it is ox-sized."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "peluda-c12",
      "source_id": "peluda-s3",
      "quote": "ペルーダ（スペイン語: la Peluda）、ヴリュ（フランス語: la velue、「毛むくじゃら」の意）は、フランス北部サルト県に伝わる怪物。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jepang menyebut peluda atau velue, \"yang berbulu lebat\", sebagai monster yang diceritakan di departemen Sarthe, Prancis utara.",
        "en": "The Japanese article calls the peluda or velue, 'the shaggy one', a monster told of in the Sarthe department of northern France."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sugaar atau Maju adalah dewa ular atau naga dalam mitologi Basque, pasangan dewi Mari, yang bersatu dengannya setiap Jumat untuk menimbulkan badai.",
    "en": "Sugaar or Maju is a serpent or dragon god of Basque mythology, consort of the goddess Mari, who joins her every Friday to raise storms.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sugaar-c01",
      "sugaar-c02",
      "sugaar-c09"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sugaar, juga Sugar, Sugoi, atau Maju, \"ular jantan\", adalah separuh laki-laki dari dewa Basque pra-Kristen yang berkaitan dengan badai dan guntur. Wujud aslinya ular atau naga, kadang api atau petir. Ia pasangan Mari dan mengunjunginya di Anboto setiap Jumat; pertemuan mereka melahirkan badai dan hujan es dan melambangkan hujan yang menyuburkan bumi. Ia tinggal di gua dan gunung seperti Aralar dan ayah Mikelatz dan Atarrabi.",
      "en": "Sugaar, also Sugar, Sugoi or Maju, 'male serpent', is the male half of a pre-Christian Basque deity of storms and thunder. His true form is a serpent or dragon, sometimes fire or lightning. He is Mari's consort and visits her at Anboto every Friday; their union brings storms and hail and figures the rain that fertilises the earth. He lives in caves and mountains such as Aralar and fathered Mikelatz and Atarrabi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sugaar-c01",
        "sugaar-c02",
        "sugaar-c03",
        "sugaar-c04",
        "sugaar-c08",
        "sugaar-c09",
        "sugaar-c11",
        "sugaar-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Betelu ia dianggap roh jahat dan di Ataun momok. Legenda dari Dima menceritakan dua bersaudara yang menemukannya di gua Baltzola; si adik memotong ekornya, dan bertahun-tahun kemudian sabuk merah pemberian Sugaar membakar pohon kastanye. Mitos lain menjadikannya ayah Jaun Zuria, penguasa pertama Biscaya, dari seorang putri Skotlandia.",
      "en": "In Betelu he is an evil spirit and in Ataun a bogeyman. A legend from Dima tells of two brothers who found him in the Baltzola cave; the younger cut his tail, and years later a red belt from Sugaar set a chestnut tree ablaze. Another myth makes him father, by a Scottish princess, of Jaun Zuria, first lord of Biscay.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sugaar-c05",
        "sugaar-c06",
        "sugaar-c07",
        "sugaar-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "title": "Sugaar",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",

    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugaar",
      "title": "Sugaar",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-s3",
      "url": "https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugaar_(mitolog%C3%ADa)",
      "title": "Sugaar (mitología)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (es)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "es",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c01",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "In Basque mythology, Sugaar (also Sugar, Sugoi, Suarra, Maju) is the male half of a pre-Christian Basque deity associated with storms and thunder. He shows may similarities to Herensuge, with Sugaar being much older.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Basque, Sugaar (juga Sugar, Sugoi, Suarra, Maju) adalah separuh laki-laki dari dewa Basque pra-Kristen yang berkaitan dengan badai dan guntur, dan ia banyak mirip Herensuge meski jauh lebih tua.",
        "en": "In Basque mythology Sugaar (also Sugar, Sugoi, Suarra, Maju) is the male half of a pre-Christian Basque deity of storms and thunder, much like Herensuge but far older."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c02",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "Sugaar is sometimes said to take the form of pure fire or lightning, but his true form is usually represented as a serpent or dragon. He is Mari's consort, and is said to visit her at Anboto every Friday.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sugaar kadang berwujud api murni atau petir, tetapi wujud aslinya biasanya ular atau naga; ia pasangan Mari dan konon mengunjunginya di Anboto setiap Jumat.",
        "en": "Sugaar sometimes takes the form of pure fire or lightning, but his true form is usually a serpent or dragon; he is Mari's consort and visits her at Anboto every Friday."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c03",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "The visits are also considered sexual in nature, a reference to how the rain (Sugaar) fertilizes the earth and soil (Mari).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kunjungan itu juga dianggap bersifat seksual, melambangkan hujan (Sugaar) yang menyuburkan bumi dan tanah (Mari).",
        "en": "The visits are also seen as sexual, a figure of the rain (Sugaar) fertilising the earth (Mari)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c04",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "Like Mari, Sugar is said to live in various caves and mountains. One of his homes is said to be the Aralar Mountain Range.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seperti Mari, Sugaar tinggal di berbagai gua dan gunung, salah satunya Pegunungan Aralar.",
        "en": "Like Mari he lives in various caves and mountains, one of them the Aralar range."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c05",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "In Betelu, Sugaar is said to be an evil spirit, and in Ataun, he is used as a bogyman figure.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Betelu Sugaar dianggap roh jahat, dan di Ataun ia dipakai sebagai momok untuk menakuti anak.",
        "en": "In Betelu Sugaar is an evil spirit, and in Ataun a bogeyman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c06",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "The pair found Sugaar in a cave in Baltzola, and the younger of the two cut his tail with a stone.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebuah legenda, dua bersaudara dari Dima menemukan Sugaar di gua Baltzola, dan si adik memotong ekornya dengan batu.",
        "en": "In one legend two brothers from Dima found Sugaar in the Baltzola cave, and the younger cut his tail with a stone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c07",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "When the brothers met again, the younger brother refused to put the belt on and tied it to a chestnut tree. The tree burst into flames, and they realized that the man must have been Sugaar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bertahun-tahun kemudian si adik menolak memakai sabuk merah pemberian seorang lelaki misterius dan mengikatkannya ke pohon kastanye, yang langsung terbakar, sehingga mereka sadar lelaki itu Sugaar.",
        "en": "Years later the younger brother refused a red belt from a mysterious man and tied it to a chestnut tree, which burst into flames, and they realised the man was Sugaar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c08",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s1",
      "quote": "The name Suga(a)r is derived from suge (serpent) and -ar (male), thus \"male serpent\". The suggestions of a formation based on su (fire) and gar (flame), thus yielding \"flame of fire\" are considered folk etymology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Sugaar berasal dari suge (ular) dan -ar (jantan), jadi \"ular jantan\"; tafsiran su (api) + gar (nyala), \"nyala api\", dianggap etimologi rakyat.",
        "en": "Sugaar comes from suge (serpent) and -ar (male), 'male serpent'; the reading su (fire) + gar (flame), 'flame of fire', is considered folk etymology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c09",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s2",
      "quote": "L'essentiel de son existence est de se joindre périodiquement (le vendredi, à deux heures de l'après-midi) à Mari dans les montagnes pour y engendrer des orages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, tugas utamanya adalah bersatu secara berkala dengan Mari di gunung (hari Jumat pukul dua siang) untuk melahirkan badai.",
        "en": "According to the French article, his main role is to join Mari periodically in the mountains (on Friday at two in the afternoon) to engender storms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c10",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s2",
      "quote": "Additionnellement, il existe un mythe dans lequel il séduit une princesse écossaise dans le village de Mundaka, pour concevoir le mythique premier seigneur de Biscaye, Jaun Zuria.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada pula mitos bahwa ia menggoda seorang putri Skotlandia di desa Mundaka dan menjadi ayah Jaun Zuria, penguasa pertama Biscaya yang mitis.",
        "en": "There is also a myth that he seduced a Scottish princess at Mundaka and fathered Jaun Zuria, the mythical first lord of Biscay."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c11",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s2",
      "quote": "Maju est considéré comme un génie souterrain qui, en sortant de son antre, s’unit à Mari, provoquant alors une tempête accompagnée de grêle et de grêlons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maju dianggap roh bawah tanah yang, ketika keluar dari sarangnya dan bersatu dengan Mari, menimbulkan badai disertai hujan es.",
        "en": "Maju is considered an underground spirit who, leaving his lair to unite with Mari, brings a storm with hail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sugaar-c12",
      "source_id": "sugaar-s3",
      "quote": "Es una divinidad de carácter masculino, consorte de la diosa Mari (tiene, sin embargo, un papel mucho más oscuro que esta), y padre de Mikelatz (hijo perverso) y Atarrabi (hijo bondadoso).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, ia dewa laki-laki, pasangan dewi Mari dengan peran yang lebih samar, dan ayah Mikelatz (anak jahat) serta Atarrabi (anak baik).",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, he is a male deity, consort of the goddess Mari though with a much dimmer role, and father of Mikelatz (the wicked son) and Atarrabi (the good son)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan es; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {

    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Badalisc adalah makhluk mitis bertanduk dan bermata menyala dari Val Camonica, Italia, yang setiap malam Epifani ditangkap, dibawa ke desa Andrista untuk \"membeberkan\" dosa warga, lalu dilepaskan kembali ke hutan.",
    "en": "The Badalisc is a horned, glowing-eyed mythical creature of Val Camonica, Italy, captured each Epiphany eve and brought to Andrista to 'reveal' the villagers' sins, then released to the woods.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Badalisc adalah makhluk mitis dari Val Camonica, Italia, berkepala besar berbalut kulit kambing, bertanduk kecil, bermulut besar, dan bermata menyala. Menurut legenda ia tinggal di hutan sekitar Andrista dan mengganggu warga, sehingga setiap malam Epifani (5 Januari) ia ditangkap dan dituntun ke desa oleh tokoh-tokoh bertopeng, termasuk gadis yang menjadi umpan, penyihir berkendang, dan seorang bungkuk yang berduel dengannya.",
      "en": "The Badalisc is a mythical creature of Val Camonica, Italy, with a big goatskin-covered head, small horns, a huge mouth and glowing eyes. By legend it lives in the woods around Andrista and pesters the villagers, so every Epiphany eve (5 January) it is captured and led into the village by masked figures, including a maiden as bait, drum-beating witches and a hunchback who duels it.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "badalisc-c08",

      "id": "Karena Badalisc bisu, seorang penerjemah membacakan pidatonya yang berima, membeberkan dosa dan tipu muslihat warga, lalu warga bernyanyi, menari, dan makan polenta Badalisc. Dulu perayaan ini khusus laki-laki, dan perempuan yang melihatnya ditolak menerima Komuni. Pada hari kedua Badalisc dilepaskan kembali ke hutan.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c01",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "is a mythical creature of the Val Camonica, Italy, in the southern central Alps. The Badalisc is represented today as a creature with a big head covered with a goat skin, two small horns, a huge mouth and glowing eyes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Badalisc adalah makhluk mitis dari Val Camonica, Italia, di Alpen tengah selatan, yang kini digambarkan berkepala besar berbalut kulit kambing, bertanduk dua kecil, bermulut sangat besar, dan bermata menyala.",
        "en": "The Badalisc is a mythical creature of the Val Camonica, Italy, in the southern central Alps, shown today with a big goatskin-covered head, two small horns, a huge mouth and glowing eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c02",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "According to legend the Badalisc lives in the woods around the village of Andrista (commune of Cevo) and is supposed to annoy the community: each year it is captured during Epiphany's eve (5th January, the Twelfth Night)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Badalisc tinggal di hutan sekitar desa Andrista (Cevo) dan mengganggu warga, sehingga setiap tahun ia ditangkap pada malam Epifani (5 Januari).",
        "en": "By legend the Badalisc lives in the woods around Andrista (Cevo) and pesters the community, so each year it is captured on Epiphany eve (5 January)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c03",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "led on a rope into the village by masked characters, including il domatore, gli spazzavia (the sweepers), il vecchio (the old man), la vecchia (the old woman) and the young signorina (the maiden), who is \"bait\" for the animal's lust, and the hunters.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dituntun dengan tali ke desa oleh tokoh-tokoh bertopeng, antara lain penjinak, para penyapu, lelaki tua, perempuan tua, pemburu, dan gadis muda yang menjadi \"umpan\" bagi nafsu binatang itu.",
        "en": "It is led on a rope into the village by masked figures, including the tamer, the sweepers, the old man, the old woman, the hunters and the young maiden who is 'bait' for the animal's lust."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c04",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "The Badalisc itself is a dumb creature, so the speech, nowadays written in rhyme, is read by an \"interpreter\". Once improvised, now written in advance, the speech reveals all the supposed sins and scheming of the community.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Badalisc sendiri bisu, sehingga pidatonya, kini ditulis berima, dibacakan oleh seorang \"penerjemah\"; pidato itu membeberkan dosa dan tipu muslihat warga.",
        "en": "The Badalisc is mute, so its speech, now written in rhyme, is read by an 'interpreter'; it exposes the community's supposed sins and schemes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c05",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "In the evening the community eats the \"Badalisc polenta\" (a commercial version of this traditional food was launched in 2010).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam hari warga makan \"polenta Badalisc\", yang versi komersialnya diluncurkan pada 2010.",
        "en": "In the evening the community eats 'Badalisc polenta', a commercial version of which was launched in 2010."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c06",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s1",
      "quote": "On the second day, at the end of the exhibition, the Badalisc is set free and allowed to return to the woods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada hari kedua, di akhir pertunjukan, Badalisc dilepaskan dan dibiarkan kembali ke hutan.",
        "en": "On the second day, at the end of the show, the Badalisc is freed and returns to the woods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c07",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s2",
      "quote": "si une femme venait à voir le Badalisc ou à entendre son discours, elle se voyait refuser la Sainte Communion le lendemain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, perayaan ini dulu khusus laki-laki: perempuan yang melihat Badalisc atau mendengar pidatonya akan ditolak menerima Komuni Kudus keesokan harinya.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the celebration was once for men only: a woman who saw the Badalisc or heard its speech would be refused Holy Communion the next day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c08",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s2",
      "quote": "Y participent aussi des sorcières munies de tambours, des bergers barbus, et un bossu (un torvo gobetto) qui affronte cet animal dans un duel rustique.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikut serta pula penyihir berkendang, gembala berjanggut, dan seorang bungkuk yang berduel kasar dengan binatang itu.",
        "en": "Also taking part are witches with drums, bearded shepherds and a hunchback who fights the animal in a rustic duel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "badalisc-c09",
      "source_id": "badalisc-s3",
      "quote": "La festa del Badalisc si svolge esclusivamente nella frazione di Andrista ed è una tradizione non diffusa in altre località della Valle Camonica. Ha luogo la sera del 5 gennaio, nella dodicesima notte dopo Natale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, pesta Badalisc hanya diadakan di Andrista dan tidak dikenal di tempat lain di Valle Camonica, pada malam 5 Januari, malam kedua belas setelah Natal.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, the Badalisc festival is held only in Andrista, not elsewhere in the Valle Camonica, on the evening of 5 January, the twelfth night after Christmas."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jean de l'Ours adalah pahlawan dongeng setengah beruang setengah manusia, anak seekor beruang dan seorang perempuan, yang dengan tongkat besinya menyelamatkan tiga putri dari dunia bawah.",
    "en": "Jean de l'Ours is the half-bear, half-human hero of a folktale, son of a bear and a woman, who with his iron cane rescues three princesses from an underworld.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jean-de-l-ours-c02",
      "jean-de-l-ours-c03",
      "jean-de-l-ours-c11"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jean de l'Ours atau John the Bear adalah tokoh utama dongeng Tipe 301B, salah satu dongeng paling populer dalam tradisi Hispanik dan Frankofon dan sangat dikenal di Pyrenees. Ia lahir dari seorang perempuan yang diculik dan dikurung seekor beruang di guanya, tubuhnya berbulu, dan kekuatannya luar biasa; dalam satu versi Meksiko ia manusia di atas pinggang dan beruang di bawahnya.",
      "en": "Jean de l'Ours or John the Bear is the hero of tale Type 301B, one of the most popular tales in Hispanic and Francophone tradition and very well known in the Pyrenees. He is born to a woman abducted and kept in a cave by a bear, his body is hairy and his strength superhuman; in one Mexican version he is human above the waist and bear below.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jean-de-l-ours-c01",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c05",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c06",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c07",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c10",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c11",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dengan tongkat besi yang berat ia berkelana bersama dua atau tiga kawan, mengalahkan lawan di sebuah kastel, turun ke dunia bawah, dan menyelamatkan tiga putri, lalu dikhianati kawan-kawannya, lolos, dan menikahi putri tercantik. Sebagai Juan Oso, mitos ini tersebar di dunia berbahasa Spanyol dengan lebih dari 300 versi, termasuk dalam bahasa Quechua.",
      "en": "With a heavy iron cane he travels with two or three companions, defeats a foe at a castle, descends to an underworld and rescues three princesses, is betrayed by his companions, escapes and marries the fairest princess. As Juan Oso the myth has spread across the Spanish-speaking world in over 300 versions, including in Quechua.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jean-de-l-ours-c02",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c03",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c04",
        "jean-de-l-ours-c08",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c01",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "or John the Bear, John of the Bear, John-of-the-Bear, John Bear, is the leading character in the French folktale Jean de l'Ours classed as Type 301B in the Aarne–Thompson system; it can also denote any tale of this type.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jean de l'Ours atau John the Bear adalah tokoh utama dongeng Prancis Jean de l'Ours yang digolongkan Tipe 301B dalam sistem Aarne–Thompson; nama itu juga dipakai untuk semua dongeng bertipe ini.",
        "en": "Jean de l'Ours or John the Bear is the hero of the French folktale Jean de l'Ours, classed as Type 301B in the Aarne–Thompson system; the name also denotes any tale of this type."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c02",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "Some typical elements are that the hero is born half-bear, half-human; he obtains a weapon, usually a heavy iron cane, and on his journey; he bands up with two or three companions.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Unsur khasnya: sang pahlawan lahir setengah beruang setengah manusia, mendapat senjata, biasanya tongkat besi yang berat, dan dalam perjalanannya bergabung dengan dua atau tiga kawan.",
        "en": "Typically the hero is born half-bear, half-human, gets a weapon, usually a heavy iron cane, and on his journey joins two or three companions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c03",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "At a castle the hero defeats an adversary, pursues him to a hole, discovers an underworld, and rescues three princesses. The companions abandon him in the hole, taking the princesses for themselves.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sebuah kastel ia mengalahkan seorang lawan, mengejarnya ke sebuah lubang, menemukan dunia bawah, dan menyelamatkan tiga putri, tetapi kawan-kawannya meninggalkannya di lubang dan merebut para putri.",
        "en": "At a castle he defeats a foe, chases him down a hole, finds an underworld and rescues three princesses, but his companions abandon him in the hole and take the princesses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c04",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "The hero escapes, finds the companions and gets rid of them. He marries the most beautiful princess of the three, but not before going through certain ordeal(s) by the king.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lolos, menemukan dan menyingkirkan kawan-kawannya, lalu menikahi putri tercantik setelah menjalani ujian dari raja.",
        "en": "He escapes, finds and disposes of the companions, and marries the fairest princess after ordeals set by the king."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c05",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "The character is said to be one of \"the most popular tale-types in Hispanic and Francophone tradition\". Numerous variants exist in France, often retaining the name Jean de l'Ours or something similar for the hero.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh ini disebut salah satu \"tipe dongeng paling populer dalam tradisi Hispanik dan Frankofon\", dengan banyak varian di Prancis yang sering tetap menamai pahlawannya Jean de l'Ours.",
        "en": "The tale is called one of 'the most popular tale-types in Hispanic and Francophone tradition', with many French variants often keeping the hero's name Jean de l'Ours."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c06",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "Several French versions explicitly comment on Jean de l'ours being covered with body hair on his entire body.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa versi Prancis menyebut tubuh Jean de l'Ours tertutup bulu seluruhnya.",
        "en": "Several French versions say Jean de l'Ours was covered in hair all over his body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c07",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s1",
      "quote": "The hero is human from the waist up and bear from the waist down in one Mexican version (Juan el Oso) as well as the Russian tale",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu versi Meksiko (Juan el Oso) dan dongeng Rusia \"Ivanko Medvedko\", pahlawan itu manusia dari pinggang ke atas dan beruang dari pinggang ke bawah.",
        "en": "In one Mexican version (Juan el Oso) and the Russian tale 'Ivanko Medvedko', the hero is human above the waist and bear below."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c08",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s2",
      "quote": "Juan Oso („Johann Bär“), der Sohn des Bären (Hijo del Oso, Cusco-Quechua Ukukuq wawan oder Ukuku uña, Chanka-Quechua Ukumaripa churin oder auch Maqta peludo), ist der Mythos von einem halb bärenhaften, halb menschlichen Helden",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, Juan Oso, \"Putra Beruang\" (Hijo del Oso, dalam bahasa Quechua Cusco Ukukuq wawan atau Ukuku uña), adalah mitos tentang pahlawan setengah beruang setengah manusia.",
        "en": "According to the German article, Juan Oso, 'Son of the Bear' (Hijo del Oso, Cusco Quechua Ukukuq wawan or Ukuku uña), is the myth of a half-bear, half-human hero."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c09",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s2",
      "quote": "Der Mythos ist insbesondere im spanischsprachigen Raum (Spanien, Lateinamerika, New Mexico, Kalifornien), aber auch in Teilen Asiens verbreitet. Es gibt mehr als 300 Versionen in etwa 20 Sprachen, neben dem Spanischen auch in indigenen Sprachen Amerikas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos ini tersebar terutama di dunia berbahasa Spanyol (Spanyol, Amerika Latin, New Mexico, California) dan sebagian Asia, dengan lebih dari 300 versi dalam sekitar 20 bahasa, termasuk bahasa pribumi Amerika.",
        "en": "The myth is widespread especially in the Spanish-speaking world (Spain, Latin America, New Mexico, California) and parts of Asia, with over 300 versions in about 20 languages, including indigenous American ones."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c10",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s2",
      "quote": "In einer sehr verbreiteten Version verschleppt ein Bär eine junge Frau (oft eine Prinzessin), sperrt sie in seine Höhle ein, wo er sie auch versorgt, und zeugt mit ihr einen Sohn, der halb Bär und halb Mensch ist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi yang sangat umum, seekor beruang menculik seorang perempuan muda, sering seorang putri, mengurungnya di guanya, dan memperoleh anak setengah beruang setengah manusia darinya.",
        "en": "In a very common version a bear abducts a young woman, often a princess, keeps her in his cave and fathers on her a son who is half bear and half human."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c11",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s3",
      "quote": "Jean de l’Ours est un être hybride, mi-humain, mi-animal, né d'une femme et d'un ours, et doté d'une force surhumaine qui lui permet de surmonter diverses épreuves.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Jean de l'Ours adalah makhluk campuran setengah manusia setengah binatang, lahir dari seorang perempuan dan seekor beruang, dengan kekuatan luar biasa untuk melewati berbagai ujian.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Jean de l'Ours is a hybrid, half-human and half-animal, born of a woman and a bear, with superhuman strength to overcome many trials."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jean-de-l-ours-c12",
      "source_id": "jean-de-l-ours-s3",
      "quote": "Il est particulièrement populaire dans les régions pyrénéennes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng ini sangat populer di daerah Pyrenees.",
        "en": "The tale is especially popular in the Pyrenean regions."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lamia atau lamina adalah roh perempuan cantik berkaki bebek atau ayam dari mitologi Basque yang tinggal di sungai, menyisir rambut dengan sisir emas, dan membangun jembatan pada malam hari.",
    "en": "The lamia or lamina is a beautiful Basque spirit-woman with duck or hen feet who lives in rivers, combs her hair with a golden comb and builds bridges by night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lamia-basque-mythology-c01",
      "lamia-basque-mythology-c12",
      "lamia-basque-mythology-c13",
      "lamia-basque-mythology-c14"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dikenal sebagai lamia atau lamina (jamak lamiak atau laminak), makhluk Basque ini mirip siren atau nereid dari mitologi Basque, roh alam berwujud manusia yang tinggal di sungai, mata air, dan gua dan terutama aktif malam hari. Biasanya ia perempuan cantik dengan kaki berselaput, kaki ayam, kuku kambing, atau ekor ikan yang menyisir rambut dengan sisir emas; di pesisir dikenal itsaslamiak berekor ikan, dan kadang ada lamiak laki-laki atau kurcaci yang membangun dolmen.",
      "en": "Known as lamia or lamina (plural lamiak or laminak), this Basque being resembles a siren or nereid; she is a humanlike nature spirit living in rivers, springs and caves and active mainly at night. Usually she is a beautiful woman with webbed feet, hen's feet, goat hooves or a fish tail who combs her hair with a golden comb; the coast knows fish-tailed itsaslamiak, and there are sometimes male or dwarf lamiak who build dolmens.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c01",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c02",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c07",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c09",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c10",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c11",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c12",
        "lamia-basque-mythology-c13"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lamiak murah hati kepada yang memberi mereka makanan dan membantu membajak ladang. Mereka membangun jembatan pada malam hari dan harus pergi bila belum selesai saat ayam berkokok; banyak lamiak menghilang ketika kapel dibangun di hutan. Mereka juga dipercaya berada di balik pelangi, dan banyak nama tempat seperti Lamikiz dan Lamusin berasal dari mereka.",
      "en": "Lamiak are generous to those who leave them food and help plough their fields. They build bridges by night and must leave if unfinished at cockcrow; many vanished when chapels were built in the forest. They are also believed to dwell beyond rainbows, and many place names such as Lamikiz and Lamusin come from them.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The lamia (or lamina) (plural: lamiak or laminak) is a siren or nereid-like creature in Basque mythology. Lamiak are typically portrayed as living in and around rivers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lamia atau lamina (jamak lamiak atau laminak) adalah makhluk mirip siren atau nereid dalam mitologi Basque yang biasanya tinggal di dalam dan di sekitar sungai.",
        "en": "The lamia or lamina (plural lamiak or laminak) is a siren- or nereid-like creature of Basque mythology, usually living in and around rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Mythology in coastal areas includes itsaslamiak, a variety of lamiak who live in the sea and have fish-like tails, similar to a mermaid.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di daerah pesisir dikenal itsaslamiak, lamia yang tinggal di laut dan berekor ikan seperti putri duyung.",
        "en": "Coastal areas know the itsaslamiak, sea-dwelling lamiak with fish tails like mermaids."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Basque mythology depicts lamiak as generous, aiding those who give them presents by helping them at work. For example, if a farmer were to leave food for them at the river shore, they would eat it at night and in exchange finish ploughing his field.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lamiak digambarkan murah hati dan membantu pekerjaan orang yang memberi mereka hadiah; misalnya, bila petani meninggalkan makanan di tepi sungai, mereka memakannya malam hari lalu menyelesaikan bajakan ladangnya.",
        "en": "Lamiak are generous and help with the work of those who give them gifts; if a farmer leaves food on the riverbank, they eat it at night and finish ploughing his field."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In other myths, lamiak must leave if the bridge that they were building at night remains unfinished at cockcrow. People believed that lamia ... had left a river if a stone in the bridge was missing.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos lain, lamiak harus pergi bila jembatan yang mereka bangun malam hari belum selesai saat ayam berkokok, dan batu yang hilang di jembatan dianggap tanda lamiak telah meninggalkan sungai itu.",
        "en": "In other myths lamiak must leave if the bridge they build by night is unfinished at cockcrow, and a missing stone in a bridge showed that the lamiak had left that river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Other beliefs claim that most lamiak disappeared when men built small churches in the forest.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan lain, kebanyakan lamiak menghilang ketika orang membangun kapel-kapel kecil di hutan.",
        "en": "Other beliefs hold that most lamiak vanished when people built small chapels in the forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Lamiak are also believed to be found on the other side of rainbows, where they are combing their hair. It is said that when the sunlight strikes their hair, the rainbow opens.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lamiak juga dipercaya berada di balik pelangi sambil menyisir rambut, dan pelangi terbuka ketika sinar matahari mengenai rambut mereka.",
        "en": "Lamiak are also believed to sit beyond rainbows combing their hair, and the rainbow opens when sunlight strikes it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Mythology also occasionally describes male lamiak. In those stories, they are described as strong and are attributed with the creation of dolmens at night.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ada pula lamiak laki-laki yang kuat dan dianggap membangun dolmen pada malam hari.",
        "en": "Sometimes there are male lamiak, strong and credited with building dolmens by night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Many toponyms relate to lamiak, including Lamikiz (Markina), Laminaputzu (in Zeanuri), Lamitegi (in Bedaio), Lamirain (in Arano), Lamusin (in Sare), Lamiñosin (in Ataun).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak nama tempat berkaitan dengan lamiak, seperti Lamikiz (Markina), Laminaputzu (Zeanuri), Lamusin (Sare), dan Lamiñosin (Ataun).",
        "en": "Many place names relate to lamiak, such as Lamikiz (Markina), Laminaputzu (Zeanuri), Lamusin (Sare) and Lamiñosin (Ataun)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "est le terme basque désignant un être fantastique de la mythologie basque, un esprit de la nature ou un Irelu ou génie d'apparence humaine.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, lamina adalah istilah Basque untuk makhluk fantastis, roh alam atau genius berwujud manusia.",
        "en": "According to the French article, lamina is the Basque term for a fantastic being, a nature spirit or humanlike genie."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Le plus souvent, les laminak sont dépeints soit comme des lutins mâles, des nains (ipotxak ou nanoak), soit comme des femmes de taille normale dont le bas du corps est pourvu de caractéristiques animales (pieds palmés, pattes de poules, sabots de chèvre ou queue de poisson).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laminak paling sering digambarkan sebagai kurcaci laki-laki atau perempuan bertubuh biasa yang bagian bawahnya berciri hewan: kaki berselaput, kaki ayam, kuku kambing, atau ekor ikan.",
        "en": "Laminak are most often shown either as male goblins or dwarfs or as normal-sized women whose lower body has animal features: webbed feet, hen's feet, goat hooves or a fish tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c11",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Créatures essentiellement nocturnes, les laminak vivent sous terre, dans des grottes ou auprès des sources et des ruisseaux.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laminak terutama makhluk malam yang tinggal di bawah tanah, di gua, atau dekat mata air dan sungai kecil.",
        "en": "Laminak are mainly nocturnal and live underground, in caves or by springs and streams."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c12",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Los seres humanos las ven peinándose el cabello con un peine de oro o, con menos frecuencia, tendiendo y secando su hermoso paño.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, manusia melihat mereka menyisir rambut dengan sisir emas atau, lebih jarang, menjemur kain mereka yang indah.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, people see them combing their hair with a golden comb or, less often, spreading out their beautiful cloth to dry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c13",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "En muchas narraciones se la describe como una mujer hermosa que presenta algún rasgo no humano, como patas de gallina, de cabra, de pato o cola de pez, según la zona y la tradición oral concreta.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam banyak cerita ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan cantik dengan satu ciri bukan manusia, seperti kaki ayam, kambing, atau bebek, atau ekor ikan, tergantung daerahnya.",
        "en": "In many tales she is a beautiful woman with some non-human feature, such as hen's, goat's or duck's feet or a fish tail, depending on the area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamia-basque-mythology-c14",
      "source_id": "lamia-basque-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In some places, bridges were believed to have been built at night by lamiak",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di beberapa tempat, jembatan dipercaya dibangun oleh lamiak pada malam hari.",
        "en": "In some places bridges were believed to have been built by lamiak at night."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mouros adalah bangsa makhluk gaib dalam legenda Portugal, Galisia, dan Asturias yang tinggal di bawah tanah dekat castro dan makam kuno, mengolah emas, dan menyimpan harta yang dijaga cuélebre.",
    "en": "The Mouros are a supernatural race of Portuguese, Galician and Asturian legend who live underground near castros and ancient barrows, work gold and keep treasure guarded by cuélebres.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mouros-c01",
      "mouros-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mouros atau Moiros adalah bangsa makhluk gaib yang mendiami Galisia, Asturias, dan Portugal sejak awal zaman lalu berlindung di bawah tanah, sehingga kini terlihat di sekitar castro dan makam gundukan panjang. Mereka mengolah emas, perak, dan permata menjadi harta yang dijaga cuélebre, dan jarang keluar kecuali untuk mencari makan atau pada pertengahan musim panas. Mouros laki-laki pandai emas berkulit gelap, sedangkan mouras dikenal sebagai penyihir berambut merah.",
      "en": "The Mouros or Moiros are a supernatural race who have lived in Galicia, Asturias and Portugal since the beginning of time and then took refuge underground, so they are now seen around castros and long barrows. They work gold, silver and gems into treasures guarded by cuélebres and rarely come out except for food or at Midsummer. The male mouros are dark-skinned goldsmiths, while the mouras are reputed red-haired sorceresses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mouros-c01",
        "mouros-c02",
        "mouros-c03",
        "mouros-c08",
        "mouros-c10"

    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka berdagang dengan manusia dan membayar dengan emas yang berubah menjadi batu atau arang bila asal-usulnya diceritakan. Mouros encantados kadang muncul sebagai raksasa atau prajurit, dan termasuk mourinhos yang kecil seperti peri. Namanya dikaitkan dengan Latin mortuus, \"mati\", atau dengan ouro, \"emas\"; antropolog Galisia menganggap mereka kebalikan petani Galisia.",
      "en": "They trade with humans and pay in gold that turns to stone or coal if its origin is told. The Mouros encantados sometimes appear as giants or warriors and include the small, elf-like mourinhos.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mouros-c01",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "the Mouros or Moiros are a race of supernatural beings which have inhabited the lands of Galicia, Asturias and Portugal since the beginning of time.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Portugal, Galisia, dan Asturias, Mouros atau Moiros adalah bangsa makhluk gaib yang mendiami tanah Galisia, Asturias, dan Portugal sejak awal zaman.",
        "en": "In Portuguese, Galician and Asturian mythology, the Mouros or Moiros are a race of supernatural beings who have lived in Galicia, Asturias and Portugal since the beginning of time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c02",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "For unknown reasons they were forced to take refuge under the earth, and now they are usually seen by people in the surroundings of castros and long barrows. The Mouros work with gold, silver and gemstones with which they make up enormous treasures that are protected by cuélebres.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena sebab yang tak diketahui mereka terpaksa berlindung di bawah tanah dan kini biasanya terlihat di sekitar castro dan makam gundukan panjang; mereka mengolah emas, perak, dan permata menjadi harta besar yang dijaga cuélebre.",
        "en": "For unknown reasons they had to take refuge underground and are now usually seen around castros and long barrows; they work gold, silver and gems into huge treasures guarded by cuélebres."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c03",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "The Mouros do not usually go out of their dwellings, except for taking food, and also at certain special times such as Midsummer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mouros jarang keluar dari tempat tinggalnya kecuali untuk mengambil makanan dan pada waktu khusus seperti pertengahan musim panas.",
        "en": "The Mouros rarely leave their dwellings except to get food and at special times such as Midsummer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c04",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "Galician anthropologists had formed the theory that the Mouros are the opposite character of traditional Galician peasant.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para antropolog Galisia berteori bahwa Mouros adalah kebalikan dari petani Galisia tradisional.",
        "en": "Galician anthropologists theorised that the Mouros are the opposite of the traditional Galician peasant."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c05",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "The philologist Isodoro Millán argues that the term Moor comes from the Celtic mrvos akin to the Indo-European term mr-tuos, whence the Latin mortuus. This would relate the Moors to races already extinct, dead.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Filolog Isodoro Millán berpendapat kata ini berasal dari kata Kelt mrvos, kerabat kata Indo-Eropa mr-tuos dan Latin mortuus, sehingga Mouros dikaitkan dengan bangsa yang sudah punah atau mati.",
        "en": "The philologist Isodoro Millán derives the word from Celtic mrvos, akin to Indo-European mr-tuos and Latin mortuus, linking the Mouros to races already extinct or dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c06",
      "source_id": "mouros-s1",
      "quote": "The Mouros encantados some times appear as giants or warriors, and they include the legend of the moura encantada and the legend of the mourinhos or maruxinhos, a very small elf like people",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mouros encantados kadang muncul sebagai raksasa atau prajurit, dan mencakup legenda moura encantada serta mourinhos atau maruxinhos, bangsa mirip peri yang sangat kecil.",
        "en": "The Mouros encantados sometimes appear as giants or warriors, and include the legend of the moura encantada and the mourinhos or maruxinhos, a very small elf-like people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c07",
      "source_id": "mouros-s2",
      "quote": "Para otros su nombre está relacionado con el gallego ouro. Y es que los mouros eran criaturas que vivían en el subsuelo en guaridas y túneles bajo la tierra, donde —dependiendo de la zona— se dedicaban a la extracción del oro (de ahí el porqué de su nombre).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, sebagian orang mengaitkan namanya dengan kata Galisia ouro, \"emas\", karena Mouros tinggal di sarang dan terowongan bawah tanah dan menambang emas.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, others link the name to Galician ouro, 'gold', since the Mouros lived in underground lairs and tunnels mining gold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c08",
      "source_id": "mouros-s2",
      "quote": "Los mouros trabajaban en la orfebrería y en algunos casos eran oscuros de piel, como si fuesen gente ajena a la tierra gallega, mientras que las mouras tenían fama de hechiceras y eran pelirrojas",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mouros laki-laki bekerja sebagai pandai emas dan kadang berkulit gelap seolah orang asing, sedangkan mouras perempuan dikenal sebagai penyihir dan berambut merah.",
        "en": "The male mouros were goldsmiths and sometimes dark-skinned as if foreign, while the female mouras were reputed sorceresses with red hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c09",
      "source_id": "mouros-s2",
      "quote": "o bien realizan negocios o intercambios con los humanos de los cuales éstos son pagados con oro, excepto si cuentan a los demás el origen de su riqueza (el negocio con el mouro) en que el oro se convierte en piedras o carbón.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka berdagang dengan manusia dan membayar dengan emas, tetapi bila orang itu menceritakan asal kekayaannya, emasnya berubah menjadi batu atau arang.",
        "en": "They trade with humans and pay in gold, but if the person tells others where the wealth came from, the gold turns to stones or coal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mouros-c10",
      "source_id": "mouros-s3",
      "quote": "раса сверхъестественных существ, населяющих земли Галисии, Астурии и Португалии с начала времён.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Rusia juga menyebut Mouros bangsa makhluk gaib yang mendiami Galisia, Asturias, dan Portugal sejak awal zaman.",
        "en": "The Russian article also calls the Mouros a race of supernatural beings inhabiting Galicia, Asturias and Portugal since the beginning of time."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Asal nama Mouros",
        "en": "Origin of the name Mouros"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Dari kata Kelt mrvos, terkait Latin mortuus, \"mati\".",
            "en": "From Celtic mrvos, related to Latin mortuus, 'dead'."

            "en": "From Galician ouro, 'gold'."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

      ]

  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tartalo atau Tartaro adalah raksasa bermata satu pemakan manusia dari mitologi Basque yang tinggal di gua pegunungan, mirip Kiklops Polifemos, dan memiliki cincin ajaib yang dapat berbicara.",
    "en": "Tartalo or Tartaro is a one-eyed man-eating giant of Basque mythology who lives in mountain caves, much like the Cyclops Polyphemus, and owns a talking magic ring.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tartalo-c01",
      "tartalo-c04",
      "tartalo-c10"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tartalo, Tartaro, atau Torto adalah raksasa bermata satu yang sangat kuat dari mitologi Basque, mirip Kiklops Polifemos, yang tinggal di gua pegunungan, beternak domba, dan menangkap orang muda untuk dimakan. Ukurannya dan nafsu makannya sangat besar, dan ia gemar melempar batu antargunung. Cerquand menduga namanya dari bangsa Tatar, dan tokoh bernama Tartari juga dikenal di Agenais.",
      "en": "Tartalo, Tartaro or Torto is an enormously strong one-eyed giant of Basque mythology, much like the Cyclops Polyphemus, who lives in mountain caves, keeps sheep and catches young people to eat. His size and appetite are huge, and he loves hurling stones between mountains. Cerquand derived his name from the Tartar people, and a figure called Tartari is known in the Agenais.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tartalo-c01",
        "tartalo-c02",
        "tartalo-c03",
        "tartalo-c09",
        "tartalo-c10",
        "tartalo-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah terkenal, dua bersaudara dari Antimuño berlindung di gua Tartalo; ia memakan si kakak, lalu si adik mencuri cincinnya, membutakan matanya dengan tusuk panggangan, dan lolos berbalut kulit domba. Cincin ajaib itu berteriak memberi tahu tempatnya, maka si anak memotong jarinya dan melemparkannya ke jurang, dan Tartalo yang mengikuti suara cincin jatuh dari tebing.",
      "en": "In a well-known tale two brothers from Antimuño shelter in Tartalo's cave; he eats the elder, then the younger steals his ring, blinds him with a roasting spit and escapes in a sheepskin. The magic ring shouts out where he is, so the boy cuts off his finger and throws it into the ravine, and Tartalo, following the ring's voice, falls off the cliff.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c01",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "Tartaro, Tartalo, or Torto in Basque mythology, is an enormously strong one-eyed giant very similar to the Greek Cyclops that Odysseus faced in Homer's Odyssey. He is said to live in caves in the mountains and catches young people in order to eat them; in some accounts he eats sheep also.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tartaro, Tartalo, atau Torto dalam mitologi Basque adalah raksasa bermata satu yang sangat kuat, mirip Kiklops Yunani yang dihadapi Odisseus, yang tinggal di gua pegunungan dan menangkap orang muda untuk dimakan, kadang juga domba.",
        "en": "Tartaro, Tartalo or Torto of Basque mythology is an enormously strong one-eyed giant much like the Greek Cyclops Odysseus faced, living in mountain caves and catching young people to eat, sometimes sheep too."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c02",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "Torto, Anxo and Alarabi were the forms listed in Jose Migel Barandiaran's Basque Mythology, with \"Tartalo\" described as a local variant particular to the Zegama region.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Torto, Anxo, dan Alarabi adalah bentuk nama yang dicatat Barandiaran, dengan \"Tartalo\" sebagai varian setempat daerah Zegama.",
        "en": "Torto, Anxo and Alarabi are the forms listed by Barandiaran, with 'Tartalo' a local variant of the Zegama area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c03",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "Cerquand ... in his book \"Légendes et récits populaires du Pays basque, volume 1\" (1875) suggested in an annotation that the name \"Tartaro\" derived from the Tartar people",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerquand dalam bukunya (1875) menduga nama \"Tartaro\" berasal dari bangsa Tatar.",
        "en": "Cerquand in his 1875 book suggested that the name 'Tartaro' came from the Tartar people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c04",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "A mystical ring is a common theme in the Tartalo/Tartaro tales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cincin ajaib adalah tema umum dalam kisah-kisah Tartalo.",
        "en": "A magic ring is a common theme in the Tartalo tales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c05",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "One day, while two brothers of the Antimuño baserri were hunting, a storm broke, so they decided to take refuge from the rain in a cave, which was Tartalo's cave.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu hari dua bersaudara dari pertanian Antimuño yang sedang berburu berlindung dari badai di sebuah gua, yang ternyata gua Tartalo.",
        "en": "One day two brothers from the Antimuño farm, out hunting, sheltered from a storm in a cave that turned out to be Tartalo's."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c06",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "While he was sleeping, the youngest brother stole Tartalo's ring and then he stuck the roasting spit in his only eye.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah Tartalo memakan si kakak dan tertidur, si adik mencuri cincinnya dan menusukkan tusuk panggangan ke satu-satunya matanya.",
        "en": "After Tartalo ate the elder brother and fell asleep, the younger stole his ring and drove the roasting spit into his only eye."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c07",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "He started to look for the boy among his sheep, but he put on a sheep's skin and escaped from Tartalo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tartalo mencari si anak di antara dombanya, tetapi anak itu memakai kulit domba dan lolos.",
        "en": "Tartalo searched for the boy among his sheep, but the boy put on a sheepskin and escaped."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c08",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "The young one wasn't able to take off the ring, so, when he arrived to the edge of a cliff, he cut off his finger, and since Tartalo was near, he decided to throw it down the cliff. Tartalo, following the ring's shouting, fell off the cliff.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cincin itu berteriak memberi tahu tempatnya dan tidak dapat dilepas, maka di tepi tebing si anak memotong jarinya dan melemparkannya ke bawah; Tartalo mengikuti teriakan cincin itu dan jatuh dari tebing.",
        "en": "The ring shouted out his whereabouts and would not come off, so at a cliff edge the boy cut off his finger and threw it down; Tartalo followed the ring's shouts and fell off the cliff."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c09",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s2",
      "quote": "Un personnage appelé Tartari était attesté au-delà de la Gascogne, rive droite de la Garonne, dans l'Agenais",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, tokoh bernama Tartari juga dikenal di luar Gascogne, di Agenais di tepi kanan Garonne.",
        "en": "According to the French article, a figure called Tartari was known beyond Gascony, in the Agenais on the right bank of the Garonne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c10",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s2",
      "quote": "Les caractéristiques et les aventures qu'on lui prête correspondent d'assez près à celles du cyclope Polyphème. Comme lui il vit dans une caverne, il élève des moutons",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciri dan petualangannya cukup dekat dengan Kiklops Polifemos: seperti dia, Tartalo tinggal di gua dan beternak domba.",
        "en": "His traits and adventures closely match those of the Cyclops Polyphemus: like him he lives in a cave and keeps sheep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c11",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s1",
      "quote": "Anxo or Ancho is however explained as an alternate name for Basajaun by some sources.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian sumber menyebut Anxo atau Ancho sebagai nama lain Basajaun.",
        "en": "Some sources explain Anxo or Ancho as another name for Basajaun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tartalo-c12",
      "source_id": "tartalo-s3",
      "quote": "Su tamaño es descomunal, al igual que su apetito, y su entretenimiento favorito era tirar piedras de un monte a otro. Cuenta la leyenda que debido a este entretenimiento se crearon varias construcciones existentes hoy en día.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, ukuran dan nafsu makannya sangat besar, dan kegemarannya melempar batu dari satu gunung ke gunung lain konon menghasilkan beberapa bangunan yang masih ada.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, his size and appetite are enormous, and his favourite pastime of hurling stones from mountain to mountain is said to have created several structures still standing."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan es; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tohil adalah dewa api, hujan, dan matahari orang Maya Kʼicheʼ, dewa pelindung mereka yang menuntut korban darah dan dipuja di kuil utama Qʼumarkaj.",
    "en": "Tohil is the Kʼicheʼ Maya god of fire, rain and sun, their patron who demanded blood sacrifice and was worshipped in the great temple of Qʼumarkaj.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tohil-c01",
      "tohil-c02",
      "tohil-c05",
      "tohil-c09",
      "tohil-c14"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tohil atau Tojil adalah dewa api orang Maya Kʼicheʼ pada periode Pascaklasik Akhir, juga dewa matahari, hujan, perang, pengorbanan, dan gunung, dan dewa pelindung Kʼicheʼ pada masa penaklukan Spanyol. Menurut Popol Vuh, manusia pertama menerima Tohil di Tollan, Tempat Tujuh Gua. Ia dikaitkan dengan guntur, petir, matahari terbit, dan buntelan kulit rusa suci, dan bersama Awilix dan Jacawitz membentuk tiga serangkai dewa elite Kʼicheʼ.",
      "en": "Tohil or Tojil is the fire god of the Late Postclassic Kʼicheʼ Maya, also a god of sun, rain, war, sacrifice and mountains, and the Kʼicheʼ patron at the Spanish conquest. In the Popol Vuh the first people received Tohil at Tollan, Place of the Seven Caves. He was tied to thunder, lightning, sunrise and a sacred deerskin bundle, and with Awilix and Jacawitz formed the Kʼicheʼ elite's divine triad.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tohil-c01",
        "tohil-c02",
        "tohil-c03",
        "tohil-c04",
        "tohil-c07",
        "tohil-c08",
        "tohil-c13",
        "tohil-c14"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tohil menuntut korban darah, yang dalam Popol Vuh diibaratkan bayi menyusu; suku Kaqchikel yang menolak korban manusia mencuri api darinya. Kuil utamanya ada di Qʼumarkaj, dan para rohaniwan Spanyol berusaha memusnahkan pemujaannya. Orang Kʼicheʼ menyamakannya dengan Quetzalcoatl, dan di Santiago Atitlán ia kemungkinan bertahan dalam sosok \"Raja Martin\".",
      "en": "Tohil demanded blood sacrifice, likened in the Popol Vuh to a suckling infant; the Kaqchikel, refusing human sacrifice, stole fire from him. His main temple stood at Qʼumarkaj, and the Spanish clergy tried to stamp out his cult. The Kʼicheʼ equated him with Quetzalcoatl, and in Santiago Atitlán he probably survives as 'King Martin'.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "tohil-c06",
