  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tohil-c01",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "also spelled Tojil) is the Maya god of fire. He is a deity of the Kʼicheʼ Maya in the Late Postclassic period of Mesoamerica.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tohil (juga Tojil) adalah dewa api Maya, dewa orang Maya Kʼicheʼ pada periode Pascaklasik Akhir Mesoamerika.",
        "en": "Tohil (also Tojil) is the Maya god of fire, a deity of the Kʼicheʼ Maya in the Late Postclassic period of Mesoamerica."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c02",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "At the time of the Spanish Conquest, Tohil was the patron god of the Kʼicheʼ.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada masa penaklukan Spanyol, Tohil adalah dewa pelindung orang Kʼicheʼ.",
        "en": "At the time of the Spanish Conquest, Tohil was the patron god of the Kʼicheʼ."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c03",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "Tohil was also associated with mountains and he was a god of war, sacrifice and sustenance.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tohil juga dikaitkan dengan gunung dan merupakan dewa perang, pengorbanan, dan rezeki.",
        "en": "Tohil was also linked with mountains and was a god of war, sacrifice and sustenance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c04",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "In the Kʼicheʼ epic Popul Vuh, after the first people were created, they gathered at the mythical Tollan or Tula, the Place of the Seven Caves, to receive their language and their gods. The Kʼicheʼ, and others, there received Tohil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam epos Kʼicheʼ Popol Vuh, manusia pertama berkumpul di Tollan atau Tula yang mitis, Tempat Tujuh Gua, untuk menerima bahasa dan dewa mereka, dan di sana orang Kʼicheʼ menerima Tohil.",
        "en": "In the Kʼicheʼ epic Popol Vuh the first people gathered at the mythical Tollan or Tula, Place of the Seven Caves, to receive their language and gods, and there the Kʼicheʼ received Tohil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c05",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "In the Popul Vuh this consumption of blood by Tohil is likened to the suckling of an infant by its mother.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tohil menuntut korban darah, dan dalam Popol Vuh konsumsi darah itu diibaratkan bayi yang menyusu kepada ibunya.",
        "en": "Tohil demanded blood sacrifice, and in the Popol Vuh his consumption of blood is likened to a baby suckling at its mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c06",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "Indeed, it's known that the Kaqchikel tribe was against the human sacrifice demanded by Tohil to bring them fire. For this reason they stole the fire from the deity",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suku Kaqchikel menolak korban manusia yang dituntut Tohil sebagai imbalan api, sehingga mereka mencuri api dari dewa itu.",
        "en": "The Kaqchikel refused the human sacrifice Tohil demanded in return for fire, and so they stole the fire from the god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c07",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "Tohil was one of a trinity of gods worshipped by the Kʼicheʼ elite, together with Awilix and Jacawitz.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tohil adalah salah satu dari tiga dewa yang dipuja kaum elite Kʼicheʼ, bersama Awilix dan Jacawitz.",
        "en": "Tohil was one of a trinity of gods worshipped by the Kʼicheʼ elite, with Awilix and Jacawitz."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c08",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "He was associated with a sacred deerskin bundle that was said to embody him, and one of his titles was Qajawal Kej (\"Our Lord Deer\"). The deity was associated with thunder, lightning and the sunrise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan buntelan kulit rusa suci yang dianggap perwujudannya, salah satu gelarnya Qajawal Kej (\"Tuan Rusa Kami\"), dan ia berkaitan dengan guntur, petir, dan matahari terbit.",
        "en": "He was tied to a sacred deerskin bundle said to embody him, one of his titles was Qajawal Kej ('Our Lord Deer'), and he was associated with thunder, lightning and sunrise."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c09",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "Later the Kʼicheʼ built their main temple to Tohil at Qʼumarkaj, their new capital.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian orang Kʼicheʼ membangun kuil utama Tohil di ibu kota baru mereka, Qʼumarkaj.",
        "en": "Later the Kʼicheʼ built their main temple to Tohil at their new capital, Qʼumarkaj."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c10",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "The god's association with human sacrifice meant that Tohil was one of the first deities that the Spanish clergy tried to eradicate",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena kaitannya dengan korban manusia, Tohil termasuk dewa pertama yang ingin dimusnahkan para rohaniwan Spanyol.",
        "en": "Because of his link with human sacrifice, Tohil was among the first gods the Spanish clergy tried to stamp out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c11",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "The Kʼicheʼ themselves claimed in the Popul Vuh that their patron Tohil was the same as Quetzalcoatl of the Aztecs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Kʼicheʼ sendiri menyatakan dalam Popol Vuh bahwa Tohil sama dengan Quetzalcoatl dari bangsa Aztek.",
        "en": "The Kʼicheʼ themselves claimed in the Popol Vuh that Tohil was the same as the Aztec Quetzalcoatl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c12",
      "source_id": "tohil-s1",
      "quote": "King Martin is probably a blend of Tohil and his deerskin bundle with the Roman Catholic St Martin of Tours, whose feast falls on the same day.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Santiago Atitlán, dewa \"Raja Martin\" kemungkinan perpaduan Tohil dan buntelan kulit rusanya dengan Santo Martinus dari Tours, yang hari rayanya jatuh pada hari yang sama.",
        "en": "In Santiago Atitlán, 'King Martin' is probably a blend of Tohil and his deerskin bundle with St Martin of Tours, whose feast falls on the same day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c13",
      "source_id": "tohil-s2",
      "quote": "adalah dewa Maya K'iche' pada periode Pasca-Klasik Akhir Mesoamerika. Pada masa pendudukan Spanyol, Tohil adalah dewa pelindung K'iche'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Indonesia juga menyebut Tohil dewa Maya K'iche' pada periode Pascaklasik Akhir dan dewa pelindung K'iche' pada masa pendudukan Spanyol.",
        "en": "The Indonesian article also calls Tohil a K'iche' Maya god of the Late Postclassic and their patron god at the Spanish conquest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tohil-c14",
      "source_id": "tohil-s3",
      "quote": "C'était une divinité du feu mais aussi du soleil et de la pluie. Elle était aussi associée aux montagnes et aux dieux de la guerre ainsi qu'à des sacrifices.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Tohil dewa api, matahari, dan hujan yang juga dikaitkan dengan gunung, perang, dan pengorbanan.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Tohil was a god of fire, sun and rain, also associated with mountains, war and sacrifice."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c01",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s1",
      "quote": "Atlatonin o Atlatonan era una diosa mexica de las costas y diosa madre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, Atlatonin atau Atlatonan adalah dewi Meksika (Aztek) penguasa pesisir dan dewi ibu.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, Atlatonin or Atlatonan was a Mexica goddess of the coasts and a mother goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c02",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s1",
      "quote": "Está asociada con Tezcatlipoca y algunos mitos la consideran una de sus esposas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan Tezcatlipoca, dan sebagian mitos menganggapnya salah satu istrinya.",
        "en": "She is associated with Tezcatlipoca, and some myths make her one of his wives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c03",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s1",
      "quote": "Derivado de la raíz Atl produce también Atlacamani, la diosa de las tempestades oceánicas y Atlacoya la diosa de las sequías.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari akar kata Atl (\"air\") juga lahir nama Atlacamani, dewi badai laut, dan Atlacoya, dewi kemarau.",
        "en": "From the same root Atl ('water') come Atlacamani, goddess of ocean storms, and Atlacoya, goddess of drought."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c04",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s2",
      "quote": "Атлатонин (аст. Atlatonin) — одно из имён ацтекской богини-матери.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Rusia, Atlatonin adalah salah satu nama dewi ibu Aztek.",
        "en": "According to the Russian article, Atlatonin is one of the names of the Aztec mother goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c05",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s3",
      "quote": "Atlatonin nella mitologia azteca, era una divinità madre e dea delle coste.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, Atlatonin dalam mitologi Aztek adalah dewi ibu dan dewi pesisir.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, in Aztec mythology Atlatonin was a mother goddess and goddess of the coasts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atlatonin-c06",
      "source_id": "atlatonin-s3",
      "quote": "Amava i rituali e pretendeva sacrifici. Per questo motivo veniva spesso impersonificata da giovani vergini. Conosciuta anche col nome di Atlatonal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyukai ritual dan menuntut korban, sehingga sering diperankan oleh gadis-gadis muda, dan juga dikenal dengan nama Atlatonal.",
        "en": "She loved rituals and demanded sacrifices, so she was often impersonated by young virgins, and was also known as Atlatonal."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Guivre atau vouivre adalah naga berwujud ular bernapas racun dari legenda Prancis abad pertengahan, agresif tetapi takut pada manusia telanjang.",
    "en": "The guivre or vouivre is a serpentine, venom-breathing dragon of medieval French legend, aggressive yet afraid of naked humans.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "guivre-c01",
      "guivre-c02",
      "guivre-c05"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Guivre, givre, atau vouivre adalah nama Prancis untuk makhluk mitis berwujud ular yang setara dengan lindworm atau wyvern; namanya berasal dari kata Latin vipera, dan kata Inggris wyvern berasal darinya. Ia bertubuh panjang seperti ular dan berkepala naga, kadang bertanduk, bernapas racun, dan tinggal di kolam, danau, hutan, dan tempat lembap. Ia sangat agresif tetapi tersipu dan memalingkan muka bila melihat manusia telanjang.",
      "en": "Guivre, givre or vouivre is the French name for a serpentine mythical creature equivalent to a lindworm or wyvern; the name comes from Latin vipera, and English wyvern comes from it. It has a long serpentine body and dragon's head, sometimes horns, venomous breath, and lives in pools, lakes, forests and damp places. It is highly aggressive yet blushes and looks away on seeing a naked human.",
      "claim_ids": [

Dalam kisah Santo Samson dari Dol, Santo Suliao mengeluarkan seekor guivre kecil dari jubah imam yang tamak, lalu mengusirnya.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "guivre-c01",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "Guivre (or givre) and vouivre are French names for a type of serpentine mythical creature, by extension a dragon, by some accords, equivalent to a lindworm or wyvern.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guivre (atau givre) dan vouivre adalah nama Prancis untuk sejenis makhluk mitis berwujud ular, lebih luas lagi naga, yang menurut sebagian pihak setara dengan lindworm atau wyvern.",
        "en": "Guivre (or givre) and vouivre are French names for a serpentine mythical creature, by extension a dragon, by some accounts equivalent to a lindworm or wyvern."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c02",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "In legend they are portrayed as serpentine creatures who possessed venomous breath and prowled the countryside of Medieval France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda mereka digambarkan sebagai makhluk mirip ular bernapas racun yang berkeliaran di pedesaan Prancis abad pertengahan.",
        "en": "In legend they are serpentine creatures with venomous breath that prowled the countryside of medieval France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c03",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "The English wyvern (a type of dragon) derives from Old French \"vouivre\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Inggris wyvern berasal dari kata Prancis Kuno vouivre.",
        "en": "The English word wyvern derives from Old French vouivre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c04",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "Guivres (alternatively givres) were said to possess a long, serpentine body and a dragon's head. The hind feet are not as visible if present at all. The guivre had horns in its forehead in some accounts, as well.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guivre bertubuh panjang seperti ular dan berkepala naga, kaki belakangnya hampir tak terlihat kalaupun ada, dan dalam sebagian kisah bertanduk di dahi.",
        "en": "Guivres had a long serpentine body and a dragon's head, hind feet barely visible if present at all, and in some accounts horns on the forehead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c05",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "Locally in France, it was known as an extremely aggressive creature that would sometimes attack without being provoked. They were afraid of naked humans, and when saw them, blushed and looked away.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Prancis ia dikenal sangat agresif dan kadang menyerang tanpa sebab, tetapi takut pada manusia telanjang: bila melihatnya, guivre tersipu dan memalingkan muka.",
        "en": "Locally in France it was known as extremely aggressive, sometimes attacking unprovoked, yet it feared naked humans and would blush and look away on seeing one."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c06",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "Documentation points to their residence as being in small bodies of water like pools and lakes, forests, and any damp place.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guivre tinggal di perairan kecil seperti kolam dan danau, di hutan, dan di tempat lembap.",
        "en": "Guivres lived in small bodies of water like pools and lakes, in forests and in any damp place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c07",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "Samson of Dol was present at an encounter between a small dragon-like creature, known as La Guivre (\"the guivre\"), and a priest.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Santo Samson dari Dol menyaksikan pertemuan seorang imam dengan makhluk kecil mirip naga yang disebut La Guivre.",
        "en": "Samson of Dol witnessed an encounter between a priest and a small dragon-like creature called La Guivre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c08",
      "source_id": "guivre-s1",
      "quote": "He admonished the priest and removed a hideous serpentine creature from the robe. There, he exorcised it and then compelled another man to throw it from the roof of a building in Garot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Santo Suliao menegur imam yang menyembunyikan roti di jubahnya, mengeluarkan makhluk ular yang mengerikan dari jubah itu, mengusirnya, lalu menyuruh orang lain melemparkannya dari atap sebuah bangunan di Garot.",
        "en": "Saint Suliao rebuked the priest who had hidden bread in his robe, pulled a hideous serpentine creature from it, exorcised it and had another man throw it from a roof in Garot."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c09",
      "source_id": "guivre-s2",
      "quote": "La vouivre est une créature légendaire présente dans plusieurs pays européens, ayant généralement la forme d'un dragon bipède ou d'un serpent ailé. Elle est souvent censée porter une escarboucle sur le front. En héraldique, elle est aussi appelée guivre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, vouivre adalah makhluk legenda di beberapa negara Eropa, biasanya berupa naga berkaki dua atau ular bersayap yang sering membawa batu merah delima di dahinya, dan dalam heraldik disebut guivre.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the vouivre is a legendary creature of several European countries, usually a two-legged dragon or winged serpent often bearing a carbuncle on its brow, called guivre in heraldry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c10",
      "source_id": "guivre-s2",
      "quote": "Ce mot est un régionalisme linguistique propre à la Lorraine, la Bourgogne, la Franche-Comté, le Jura et la Suisse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata vouivre adalah regionalisme khas Lorraine, Bourgogne, Franche-Comté, Jura, dan Swiss.",
        "en": "The word vouivre is a regionalism of Lorraine, Burgundy, Franche-Comté, the Jura and Switzerland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guivre-c11",
      "source_id": "guivre-s3",
      "quote": "Todas estas formas se derivan en última instancia del latín Vipera, como es el Español víbora.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, semua bentuk ini akhirnya berasal dari kata Latin vipera, seperti kata Spanyol víbora.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, all these forms ultimately come from Latin vipera, like Spanish víbora."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nuberu, \"Si Pembuat Awan\", adalah roh atau dewa awan dan badai dalam mitologi Asturias, Cantabria, Galisia, dan León yang merusak panen dengan hujan es dan petir tetapi baik kepada penolongnya.",
    "en": "Nuberu, 'The Clouder', is a spirit or god of clouds and storms in Asturian, Cantabrian, Galician and Leonese mythology who wrecks harvests with hail and lightning but is kind to those who help him.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nuberu-c01",
      "nuberu-c02",
      "nuberu-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nuberu atau Nubeiro, \"Si Pembuat Awan\", adalah dewa awan dan badai dari Asturias, Cantabria, Galisia, dan León. Di Asturias ia satu sosok tua berjanggut berpakaian kulit binatang gelap dan bertopi besar, naik kereta yang ditarik serigala, dan bermata satu; di Cantabria nuberu adalah banyak makhluk kecil gemuk bersayap hitam, dan di Galisia lelaki kecil berbulu berekor atau raksasa yang menempa petir di bengkel pegunungan. Wajahnya buruk dengan mata seperti bara.",
      "en": "Nuberu or Nubeiro, 'The Clouder', is a god of clouds and storms of Asturias, Cantabria, Galicia and León. In Asturias he is a single old bearded being in dark pelts and a big hat who rides a wolf-drawn chariot and has one eye; in Cantabria the Nuberus are many small chubby black-winged creatures, and in Galicia small hairy tailed men or a giant who forges lightning in a mountain workshop. His face is ugly with ember-like eyes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nuberu-c01",
        "nuberu-c02",
        "nuberu-c03",
        "nuberu-c04",
        "nuberu-c11",

        "nuberu-c13",
        "nuberu-c14"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia merusak ladang dan dipersalahkan nelayan atas angin kencang Laut Cantabria, tetapi baik kepada yang menolongnya: petani yang menampungnya diberi hujan dan panen, lalu dibawa menunggang awan dari Mesir pulang tepat waktu untuk menggagalkan pernikahan istrinya. Ia tinggal di Orito, Mesir, dan dianggap sisa dewa Taranis, serupa Entiznáu dari Extremadura, dan dibandingkan dengan Odin.",
      "en": "He damages fields and fishermen blame him for the Cantabrian Sea's gales, but he is kind to helpers: the peasant who sheltered him got rain and harvests and was later carried on the clouds from Egypt in time to stop his wife's wedding. He lives in Orito, Egypt, and is seen as a remnant of Taranis, akin to the Entiznáu of Extremadura, and compared with Odin.",

      "title": "Нуберу",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c01",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "Nubero (Castilian) or Nubeiro (Galician) -literally \"The Clouder\"- is a character of Asturian, Cantabrian, Galician and Leonese mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nuberu, Nubero (Kastilia), atau Nubeiro (Galisia), harfiah \"Si Pembuat Awan\", adalah tokoh mitologi Asturias, Cantabria, Galisia, dan León.",
        "en": "Nuberu, Nubero (Castilian) or Nubeiro (Galician), literally 'The Clouder', is a figure of Asturian, Cantabrian, Galician and Leonese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c02",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "According to Asturian mythology, the Nuberu (also known in Western Asturias as Reñubeiru or Xuan Cabritu) is the divinity of clouds and storms.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitologi Asturias, Nuberu (di Asturias barat juga Reñubeiru atau Xuan Cabritu) adalah dewa awan dan badai.",
        "en": "In Asturian mythology the Nuberu (in western Asturias also Reñubeiru or Xuan Cabritu) is the god of clouds and storms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c03",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "In some stories, he is an individual; in others, the Nuberu is a species of dwarf-like beings with the power to control the weather. Sometimes, he is represented as a man with a thick beard, who wears goat leathers and a big hat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian cerita ia satu sosok, dalam cerita lain sejenis makhluk mirip kurcaci yang dapat mengendalikan cuaca, dan kadang ia digambarkan lelaki berjanggut lebat berpakaian kulit kambing dan bertopi besar.",
        "en": "In some stories he is one being, in others a species of dwarf-like weather-controlling beings, and sometimes a thick-bearded man in goatskins and a big hat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c04",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "When he is treated as a single entity, Nuberu is said to dress with dark pelts and leather, travel in a chariot pulled by wolves, and wear a patch to cover an injured or missing eye.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai satu sosok, Nuberu berpakaian kulit binatang gelap, naik kereta yang ditarik serigala, dan memakai penutup mata untuk mata yang terluka atau hilang.",
        "en": "As a single being, Nuberu wears dark pelts, rides a wolf-drawn chariot and wears a patch over an injured or missing eye."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c05",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "He, or they, can be terribly cruel to people, damaging fields and pastures, although he can also be very kind to those who had helped him before. The myth tells us that he lives in the city of Orito, in Egypt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bisa sangat kejam, merusak ladang dan padang rumput, tetapi baik kepada orang yang pernah menolongnya, dan menurut mitos ia tinggal di kota Orito di Mesir.",
        "en": "He can be terribly cruel, damaging fields and pastures, but kind to those who once helped him, and by myth he lives in the city of Orito in Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c06",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "Folklorists think that Nuberu is an Asturian remnant of the ancient god Taranis, who also ruled over the skies and was worshipped in Asturias until the Middle Ages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para folklorist menganggap Nuberu sisa dewa kuno Taranis, penguasa langit yang dipuja di Asturias sampai Abad Pertengahan.",
        "en": "Folklorists see Nuberu as an Asturian remnant of the ancient sky god Taranis, worshipped in Asturias until the Middle Ages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c07",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "Within Spain, their closest parallel is the Entiznáu from Extremadura, another evil weather creature with dark appearance and power over storms, who shares description and clothing with Nuberu, but differs in size.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan terdekatnya di Spanyol adalah Entiznáu dari Extremadura, makhluk cuaca jahat yang serupa wujud dan pakaiannya tetapi berbeda ukuran.",
        "en": "Its closest Spanish parallel is the Entiznáu of Extremadura, an evil weather creature of similar look and dress but different size."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c08",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "In appreciation for his help, Nuberu irrigated his fields, and gave him good harvests, and has continued to provide rain to the people of the region.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon Nuberu jatuh dari awan dan hanya seorang petani yang mau menampungnya; sebagai terima kasih, Nuberu mengairi ladangnya, memberinya panen baik, dan terus memberi hujan kepada penduduk daerah itu.",
        "en": "Nuberu once fell from a cloud and only one peasant would shelter him; in thanks, Nuberu watered his fields, gave him good harvests and still brings rain to the region."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c09",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "The peasant then asked Nuberu for help and together they travel back to Asturias riding the clouds and they arrive in time to prevent the wedding.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bertahun-tahun kemudian petani itu di Mesir mendengar istrinya akan menikah lagi; ia meminta tolong Nuberu, dan mereka menunggang awan kembali ke Asturias tepat waktu untuk menggagalkan pernikahan.",
        "en": "Years later the peasant, in Egypt, heard his wife was to remarry; he asked Nuberu for help, and they rode the clouds back to Asturias in time to stop the wedding."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c10",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "Fishermen fear the Nuberu because they blame him for the strong north-western winds of the Cantabrian Sea, which forces them to return hurriedly to port, where worried people await them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para nelayan takut pada Nuberu karena menyalahkannya atas angin barat laut kencang di Laut Cantabria yang memaksa mereka bergegas pulang ke pelabuhan.",
        "en": "Fishermen fear the Nuberu, blaming him for the strong north-westerly winds of the Cantabrian Sea that drive them hurriedly back to port."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c11",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "In Cantabrian mythology, the Nuberus are often a multitude of little creatures, not a single one. They are described as small, chubby and mischievous, with devilish grins in their demonic faces and little black wings.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Cantabria, nuberu sering berupa banyak makhluk kecil yang gemuk dan nakal, berwajah iblis menyeringai dan bersayap hitam kecil.",
        "en": "In Cantabrian mythology the Nuberus are often many little creatures, small, chubby and mischievous, with devilish grins and little black wings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c12",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "In Southern Galicia, Nubeiros have the appearance of small hairy men with a long and twisted tail. They fly on grey clouds and cause dry summer storms, lightning, and similar disasters.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Galisia selatan, nubeiro berupa lelaki kecil berbulu berekor panjang dan berpilin yang terbang di atas awan kelabu dan menimbulkan badai kering musim panas dan petir.",
        "en": "In southern Galicia Nubeiros are small hairy men with long twisted tails who fly on grey clouds and cause dry summer storms and lightning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c13",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s1",
      "quote": "Nuberu forges lightning by himself in his iron workshop in the mountains, and when he has made enough he goes out and rides through the sky to cause storms and throw his creations around.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di bagian lain Galisia, Nuberu menempa petir sendiri di bengkel besinya di pegunungan, lalu menunggang langit untuk menimbulkan badai dan melemparkan petir-petirnya.",
        "en": "Elsewhere in Galicia, Nuberu forges lightning himself in his mountain ironworks, then rides the sky to cause storms and hurl his creations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c14",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s2",
      "quote": "Su apariencia varía dependiendo del lugar, pero casi siempre se describen como seres antropomorfos, envejecidos, extremadamente feos, de tez oscura, con boca y orejas grandes y barba poblada. Sus ojos hundidos lucen como brasas rojas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, wujud mereka berbeda menurut daerah, tetapi hampir selalu mirip manusia, tua, sangat buruk rupa, berkulit gelap, bermulut dan bertelinga besar, berjanggut lebat, dengan mata cekung menyala seperti bara merah.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, their look varies by place but they are almost always humanlike, aged, extremely ugly and dark-skinned, with big mouths and ears, thick beards and sunken eyes glowing like red embers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuberu-c15",
      "source_id": "nuberu-s3",
      "quote": "Также сравнивается с Одином и Зевсом по схожим причинам.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Rusia juga membandingkannya dengan Odin dan Zeus.",
        "en": "The Russian article also compares him with Odin and Zeus."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, es, dan ru; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rougarou adalah manusia serigala dalam cerita rakyat Cajun, Kreol, dan Métis yang berkeliaran di rawa-rawa Louisiana, berkepala serigala atau anjing, dan dipakai untuk menakuti anak-anak.",
    "en": "The rougarou is a werewolf of Cajun, Creole and Métis folklore that prowls the swamps of Louisiana with a wolf's or dog's head, used to frighten children into behaving.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rougarou, dari kata Prancis loup-garou, adalah makhluk mirip manusia serigala dalam cerita rakyat Cajun dan Kreol di Louisiana serta komunitas Métis dan berbahasa Prancis di Kanada. Ia bertubuh manusia berkepala serigala atau anjing dan berkeliaran di rawa sekitar Acadiana dan New Orleans, ladang tebu, dan hutan; kisah lain menjadikannya penunggang tanpa kepala atau penyihir.",
      "en": "The rougarou, from French loup-garou, is a werewolf-like creature of Cajun and Creole folklore in Louisiana and of Métis and French-speaking communities in Canada. It has a human body and a wolf's or dog's head and prowls the swamps around Acadiana and New Orleans, cane fields and woods; other tales make it a headless horseman or a witch.",
      "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Kisahnya dipakai untuk menakuti anak-anak. Menurut satu legenda kutukannya berlangsung 101 hari lalu berpindah ke orang yang darahnya ia tumpahkan, dan 13 benda kecil di depan pintu dapat menangkalnya karena ia tidak bisa menghitung lebih dari 12. Bagi orang Ojibwe, rugaru adalah lelaki berbulu pertanda bahaya. Ia muncul dalam serial Supernatural dan novel Empire of Wild.",
      "en": "Its tales were used to frighten children. One legend says its curse lasts 101 days and then passes to whoever's blood it draws, and 13 small objects by the door ward it off because it cannot count past 12.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c01",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "alternatively spelled as roux-ga-roux, rugaroo, or rugaru) is a legendary creature in Cajun and Creole diaspora and a trickster in oral traditions in Métis and Francophone communities linked to traditional concepts of the werewolf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rougarou (juga roux-ga-roux, rugaroo, atau rugaru) adalah makhluk legenda diaspora Cajun dan Kreol serta tokoh penipu dalam tradisi lisan komunitas Métis dan berbahasa Prancis, yang terkait dengan konsep manusia serigala.",
        "en": "The rougarou (also roux-ga-roux, rugaroo or rugaru) is a legendary creature of the Cajun and Creole diaspora and a trickster in Métis and Francophone oral traditions, linked to the werewolf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c02",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "Loup is French for wolf, and garou (from Frankish warulf, cognate with English werewolf) is a man who transforms into an animal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dari loup-garou: loup berarti serigala dalam bahasa Prancis, dan garou (dari kata Franka warulf, sekerabat dengan werewolf) adalah manusia yang berubah menjadi binatang.",
        "en": "The name comes from loup-garou: loup is French for wolf, and garou (from Frankish warulf, akin to werewolf) is a man who turns into an animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c03",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "According to Barry Jean Ancelet, an academic expert on Cajun folklore and professor at the University of Louisiana at Lafayette in America, the tale of the rougarou is a common legend across French Louisiana.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Barry Jean Ancelet, pakar cerita rakyat Cajun di University of Louisiana at Lafayette, kisah rougarou adalah legenda umum di seluruh Louisiana berbahasa Prancis.",
        "en": "According to Barry Jean Ancelet, Cajun folklore expert at the University of Louisiana at Lafayette, the rougarou is a common legend across French Louisiana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c04",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "In the Creole and Cajun legends, the creature is said to prowl the swamps around Acadiana and Greater New Orleans, and the sugar cane fields and woodlands of the regions. The rougarou most often is described as a creature with a human body and the head of a wolf or dog, similar to the werewolf legend.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda Kreol dan Cajun, rougarou berkeliaran di rawa-rawa sekitar Acadiana dan New Orleans serta ladang tebu dan hutan, dan biasanya bertubuh manusia berkepala serigala atau anjing.",
        "en": "In Creole and Cajun legend the rougarou prowls the swamps around Acadiana and New Orleans and the cane fields and woods, usually with a human body and a wolf's or dog's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c05",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "Often the story-telling has been used to inspire fear and obedience. One such example is stories that have been told by elders to persuade Creole and Cajun children to behave.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya sering dipakai untuk menanamkan rasa takut dan kepatuhan, misalnya oleh orang tua agar anak-anak Kreol dan Cajun berkelakuan baik.",
        "en": "The tales were often used to instil fear and obedience, for instance by elders to make Creole and Cajun children behave."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c06",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "A common blood sucking legend says that the rougarou is under the spell for 101 days. After that time, the curse is transferred from person to person when the rougarou draws another human's blood.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda pengisap darah yang umum, rougarou berada di bawah kutukan selama 101 hari, lalu kutukan berpindah ke orang lain ketika rougarou menumpahkan darahnya.",
        "en": "A common blood-drawing legend says the rougarou is under the spell for 101 days, after which the curse passes to another person whose blood the rougarou draws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c07",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "Some believe that placing 13 small objects by the door keeps the Rougarou at bay; the creature is said to be so simple-minded it can’t count beyond 12.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang percaya meletakkan 13 benda kecil di dekat pintu dapat menangkal rougarou karena ia terlalu bodoh untuk menghitung lebih dari 12.",
        "en": "Some believe that placing 13 small objects by the door keeps the rougarou away, since it is too simple to count past 12."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c08",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "Rugaru is the Ojibwe name for \"the hairy man who appears in symptom of danger or psychic disruption in the community.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rugaru adalah nama Ojibwe untuk \"lelaki berbulu yang muncul sebagai tanda bahaya atau kekacauan batin dalam komunitas\".",
        "en": "Rugaru is the Ojibwe name for 'the hairy man who appears as a sign of danger or psychic disruption in the community'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c09",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "Rougarous have been repeatedly mentioned in the American TV series Supernatural, and is the monster of the week in season 4 episode \"Metamorphosis\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rougarou berkali-kali disebut dalam serial televisi Amerika Supernatural dan menjadi monster utama episode \"Metamorphosis\" musim keempat.",
        "en": "The rougarou is repeatedly mentioned in the American TV series Supernatural and is the monster of the season 4 episode 'Metamorphosis'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c10",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s1",
      "quote": "The novel Empire of Wild (2019; Penguin Random House Canada) by Cherie Dimaline is \"inspired by the traditional Métis story of the Rogarou",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Novel Empire of Wild (2019) karya Cherie Dimaline terilhami kisah tradisional Métis tentang Rogarou.",
        "en": "Cherie Dimaline's novel Empire of Wild (2019) is inspired by the traditional Métis story of the Rogarou."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c11",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s2",
      "quote": "Le Rougarou, Roux-Ga-Roux, Rugaroo ou Rugaru est une créature lycanthrope appartenant au folklore des communautés francophones de Louisiane (États-Unis) et des Laurentides (Québec).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, rougarou adalah makhluk manusia serigala dalam cerita rakyat komunitas berbahasa Prancis di Louisiana dan Laurentides, Québec.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the rougarou is a werewolf creature in the folklore of French-speaking communities in Louisiana and the Laurentides, Quebec."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c12",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s2",
      "quote": "D'autres contes décrivent le rougarou comme un cavalier sans tête ou comme une sorcière.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menggambarkan rougarou sebagai penunggang kuda tanpa kepala atau sebagai penyihir.",
        "en": "Other tales describe the rougarou as a headless horseman or as a witch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rougarou-c13",
      "source_id": "rougarou-s3",
      "quote": "El Rougarou es una criatura legendaria en las comunidades francófonas en EEUU y Canadá, asociada a la noción europea del hombre lobo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, rougarou adalah makhluk legenda komunitas berbahasa Prancis di Amerika Serikat dan Kanada yang terkait dengan gagasan Eropa tentang manusia serigala.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the rougarou is a legendary creature of French-speaking communities in the United States and Canada, tied to the European werewolf idea."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vanapagan atau Vanatühi adalah iblis raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Estonia, seorang petani yang lebih bodoh daripada jahat dan selalu diakali pelayannya, Kaval Ants.",
    "en": "Vanapagan or Vanatühi is the giant devil of Estonian folklore, a farmer more stupid than wicked who is always outwitted by his servant Kaval Ants.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "vanapagan-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vanapagan atau Vanatühi adalah iblis atau dewa dunia bawah dalam mitologi Estonia, digambarkan sebagai petani raksasa yang lebih bodoh daripada jahat dengan ciri setan Kristen. Dalam dongeng \"raksasa bodoh\" ia selalu diakali pelayannya Kaval Ants; ia juga mencuri alat musik dewa petir Pikne dan menjadi musuh terbesar raksasa Suur Tõll dari Saaremaa. Topinya dari kuku membuatnya tak terlihat.",
      "en": "Vanapagan or Vanatühi is a devil or underworld god of Estonian mythology, pictured as a giant farmer more stupid than wicked with traits of the Christian Satan. In the 'stupid ogre' tales he is always outwitted by his servant Kaval Ants; he also steals the thunder god Pikne's instrument and is the worst enemy of the giant Suur Tõll of Saaremaa. His hat of fingernails makes him invisible.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vanapagan-c01",
        "vanapagan-c02",
        "vanapagan-c03",
        "vanapagan-c04",
        "vanapagan-c05",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah lain banyak vanapaganad tinggal di rawa, hutan, dan gua dan mengunjungi desa saat pesta. Vanapagan juga membentuk gunung dan danau dan memindahkan batu besar, tetapi kota dan jembatan yang ia bangun selalu tak selesai; ia takut pada serigala dan badai petir. Matthias Johann Eisen mempopulerkannya kembali.",
      "en": "In other tales many vanapaganad live in bogs, woods and caves and visit villages at feasts. Vanapagan also shapes mountains and lakes and moves great stones, but the towns and bridges he builds are never finished; he fears wolves and thunderstorms. Matthias Johann Eisen repopularised him.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c01",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s1",
      "quote": "In Estonian mythology, Vanatühi (\"Old empty one\", or alternatively, Vanapagan, \"Old devil\") is a/the devil or god of the underworld, a giant farmer who is more stupid than malevolent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Estonia, Vanatühi (\"Si Tua yang Kosong\") atau Vanapagan (\"Iblis Tua\") adalah iblis atau dewa dunia bawah, seorang petani raksasa yang lebih bodoh daripada jahat.",
        "en": "In Estonian mythology Vanatühi ('Old empty one') or Vanapagan ('Old devil') is a devil or god of the underworld, a giant farmer more stupid than malevolent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c02",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s1",
      "quote": "Vanapagan is the ogre character in Estonian versions of the series of internationally known folktales of the stupid ogre, tale types 1000–1199 in the Aarne–Thompson classification system. In these stories, he is outwitted by his servant Kaval Ants (Crafty Hans).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vanapagan adalah raksasa dalam versi Estonia dongeng internasional \"raksasa bodoh\" (tipe 1000–1199 Aarne–Thompson), yang selalu diakali pelayannya, Kaval Ants (\"Hans yang Cerdik\").",
        "en": "Vanapagan is the ogre in Estonian versions of the international 'stupid ogre' tales (Aarne–Thompson types 1000–1199), outwitted by his servant Kaval Ants ('Crafty Hans')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c03",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s1",
      "quote": "Among these folktales is the tale of Vanapagan stealing the musical instrument belonging to the god of lightning Pikne",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu dongengnya mengisahkan Vanapagan mencuri alat musik milik Pikne, dewa petir.",
        "en": "One tale tells of Vanapagan stealing the musical instrument of Pikne, god of lightning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c04",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s1",
      "quote": "He is also the worst enemy of Suur Tõll, a giant known in the folklore of the island Saaremaa. Tõll is decapitated during a battle with Vanatühi's forces.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga musuh terbesar Suur Tõll, raksasa dari cerita rakyat pulau Saaremaa, yang dipenggal dalam pertempuran melawan pasukan Vanatühi.",
        "en": "He is also the worst enemy of Suur Tõll, the giant of Saaremaa folklore, who is beheaded in a battle with Vanatühi's forces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c05",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s1",
      "quote": "Vanatühi sometimes wears a hat of fingernails ... that makes him invisible.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vanatühi kadang memakai topi dari kuku yang membuatnya tak terlihat.",
        "en": "Vanatühi sometimes wears a hat of fingernails that makes him invisible."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c06",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s2",
      "quote": "In anderen Erzählungen kommen Vanapaganad (im Plural) vor, die in Mooren, im Wald oder in Höhlen leben und gelegentlich (vor allem bei wichtigen Festen wie Hochzeiten oder Taufen) die Dörfer besuchen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, ada kisah tentang banyak vanapaganad yang tinggal di rawa, hutan, atau gua dan kadang mengunjungi desa, terutama saat pesta pernikahan atau pembaptisan.",
        "en": "According to the German article, other tales feature many vanapaganad living in bogs, woods or caves who sometimes visit villages, especially at weddings or christenings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c07",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s2",
      "quote": "Weiterhin tritt der Vanapagan als Gestalter der Erde auf, der Berge und Seen geschaffen hat oder große Steine verschiebt. Oft versucht er, Städte oder Brücken zu bauen; allerdings bleiben seine Arbeiten stets unvollendet. Er fürchtet Wölfe und Gewitter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vanapagan juga pembentuk bumi yang menciptakan gunung dan danau atau memindahkan batu besar, sering mencoba membangun kota atau jembatan yang selalu tak selesai, dan takut pada serigala dan badai petir.",
        "en": "Vanapagan also shapes the earth, making mountains and lakes or moving great stones, often tries to build towns or bridges that remain unfinished, and fears wolves and thunderstorms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c08",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s2",
      "quote": "Die Textausgaben des Folkloristen Matthias Johann Eisen (1857–1934) haben den Vanapagan in Estland wieder popularisiert.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terbitan teks folklorist Matthias Johann Eisen (1857–1934) mempopulerkan kembali Vanapagan di Estonia.",
        "en": "The editions of the folklorist Matthias Johann Eisen (1857–1934) repopularised Vanapagan in Estonia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c09",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s3",
      "quote": "в легендах — дураковатый великан, противник Большого Тылля, в сказках — противник своего работника Кавал-Антса. Имеет черты христианского сатаны и глупого и злого духа.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Rusia, dalam legenda ia raksasa dungu musuh Tõll Besar, dalam dongeng lawan pelayannya Kaval Ants, dengan ciri setan Kristen dan roh yang bodoh dan jahat.",
        "en": "According to the Russian article, in legends he is a dull giant, foe of Big Tõll, and in tales the opponent of his servant Kaval Ants, with traits of the Christian Satan and of a stupid, wicked spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vanapagan-c10",
      "source_id": "vanapagan-s2",
      "quote": "Der Vanapagan (estnisch Alter Heide) oder Vanatühi (Der alte Leere) ist eine Figur aus der estnischen Mythologie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jerman menerjemahkan Vanapagan sebagai \"Orang Kafir Tua\" dan Vanatühi sebagai \"Si Tua yang Kosong\".",
        "en": "The German article translates Vanapagan as 'Old Heathen' and Vanatühi as 'the Old Empty One'."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Arti nama Vanapagan",
        "en": "Meaning of the name Vanapagan"
      },
      "positions": [
        {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Akerbeltz, \"kambing jantan hitam\", adalah roh Basque pelindung ternak yang tinggal di dalam tanah, yang pada masa perburuan penyihir disamakan dengan setan pemimpin akelarre.",
    "en": "Akerbeltz, 'black billy goat', is a Basque spirit who guards livestock and lives inside the earth, equated during the witch hunts with the devil presiding over the akelarre.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "akerbeltz-c01",
      "akerbeltz-c06",
      "akerbeltz-c13"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Akerbeltz atau Aker, \"kambing jantan hitam\", adalah roh Basque yang tinggal di dalam tanah dan memiliki banyak peri sebagai pelayan. Ia dianggap dewa kuno pelindung hewan dan rumah yang dapat menyembuhkan ternak dan melambangkan kesuburan, sehingga banyak keluarga Basque memelihara kambing jantan hitam di kandang. Ia juga dapat menimbulkan badai, dan orang Pyrenees percaya janggut kambing dipakai untuk membuat hujan es.",
      "en": "Akerbeltz or Aker, 'black billy goat', is a Basque spirit who lives inside the earth with many elves as servants. He is seen as an ancient protector of animals and houses who heals livestock and embodies fertility, so many Basque families kept a black billy goat in the stable. He can also raise storms, and Pyrenean people believed a goat's beard was taken to make hail.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "akerbeltz-c01",
        "akerbeltz-c02",
        "akerbeltz-c06",
        "akerbeltz-c07",
        "akerbeltz-c09",
        "akerbeltz-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam agama Kristen ia disamakan dengan setan pemimpin akelarre, \"padang kambing\" tempat sabat penyihir. Inkuisitor Pierre de Lancre mencatat kesaksian bahwa ia berwajah dua dan memimpin parodi misa dengan persembahan telur, roti, dan uang; ia konon tinggal di gua Zugarramurdi. Namanya mirip dewa Mesir Aker, tetapi kaitannya tidak pasti.",
      "en": "Christianity identified him with the devil presiding over the akelarre, the 'goat's meadow' of the witches' sabbath. The inquisitor Pierre de Lancre recorded testimony that he had two faces and held a parody mass with offerings of eggs, bread and money; he was said to dwell in the Zugarramurdi caves. His name resembles the Egyptian god Aker, though any link is uncertain.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c01",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "Akerbeltz or Aker (from Basque aker, 'billy goat' and beltz, 'black') is a spirit in the folk mythology of the Basque people. It is said to live inside the land and is believed to have as many elves as servants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akerbeltz atau Aker (dari bahasa Basque aker, \"kambing jantan\", dan beltz, \"hitam\") adalah roh dalam mitologi rakyat Basque yang tinggal di dalam tanah dan memiliki banyak peri sebagai pelayan.",
        "en": "Akerbeltz or Aker (Basque aker, 'billy goat', and beltz, 'black') is a spirit of Basque folk mythology who lives inside the earth and has many elves as servants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c02",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "Contemporary belief holds that Akerbeltz was an ancient deity who protected animals.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kini Akerbeltz dianggap dewa kuno pelindung hewan.",
        "en": "Today Akerbeltz is thought to have been an ancient deity who protected animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c03",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "Pierre de Lancre, an inquisitor who went looking for women from Labourd and Lower Navarre who were supposed to be witches, wrote a book",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Inkuisitor Pierre de Lancre, yang memburu perempuan Labourd dan Navarra Bawah yang dituduh penyihir, menulis buku yang mencatat kesaksian tentang Akerbeltz.",
        "en": "The inquisitor Pierre de Lancre, who hunted supposed witches in Labourd and Lower Navarre, wrote a book recording testimony about Akerbeltz."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c04",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "Another witch said that he had two faces, one in front and the other one in the back.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seorang \"penyihir\" bersaksi bahwa ia berwajah dua, satu di depan dan satu di belakang.",
        "en": "One 'witch' testified that he had two faces, one in front and one behind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c05",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "As men who look for witches said, Aker gave a parody of the Catholic mass to his followers, and during the mass, they offer him eggs, bread and money.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut para pemburu penyihir, Aker memimpin parodi misa Katolik bagi pengikutnya, yang mempersembahkan telur, roti, dan uang.",
        "en": "According to the witch-hunters, Aker held a parody of the Catholic mass for his followers, who offered him eggs, bread and money."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c06",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "There were some other beliefs which say that he was the animals' and houses' protector, and he had the power for treating the animals. He was also the life image of the fertility.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan lain ia pelindung hewan dan rumah, dapat menyembuhkan hewan, dan lambang kesuburan.",
        "en": "Other beliefs make him protector of animals and houses, able to heal animals, and an image of fertility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c07",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "And as he was black, many people from the Basque Country had had a black billy-goat in their stable in order to protect the rest of the animals.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena ia hitam, banyak orang Basque memelihara kambing jantan hitam di kandang untuk melindungi hewan lainnya.",
        "en": "Because he was black, many Basques kept a black billy goat in the stable to protect the other animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c08",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "He was supposed to be in the entrance of the caves of Zugarramurdi where elves, lamia, and witches met.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia konon berada di mulut gua Zugarramurdi, tempat peri, lamia, dan penyihir berkumpul.",
        "en": "He was said to dwell at the mouth of the Zugarramurdi caves, where elves, lamiak and witches met."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c09",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "People from the Pyrenees thought that witches, Akerbeltz and the demon could go to any house and take the beard from a billy-goat in order to make hail.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Pyrenees percaya penyihir, Akerbeltz, dan setan dapat masuk ke rumah mana pun dan mengambil janggut kambing jantan untuk membuat hujan es.",
        "en": "Pyrenean people believed witches, Akerbeltz and the devil could enter any house and take a billy goat's beard to make hail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c10",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s2",
      "quote": "Aker ou Akerbeltz est un génie ou un Irelu, une divinité maléfique et souterraine dans la mythologie basque ressemblant à un bouc, capable de commander une foule de génies et de déclencher des tempêtes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Aker atau Akerbeltz adalah genius jahat bawah tanah berwujud kambing jantan yang memerintah banyak genius dan dapat menimbulkan badai.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Aker or Akerbeltz is an evil underground genie in goat form who commands many genies and can raise storms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c11",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s2",
      "quote": "On note la consonance des dieux égyptien Aker et basque Aker, sans certitude.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada kemiripan bunyi antara dewa Mesir Aker dan Aker Basque, tetapi tanpa kepastian.",
        "en": "The names of the Egyptian god Aker and the Basque Aker sound alike, though nothing is certain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c12",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s2",
      "quote": "Akelarre ou Akerlanda (lande du bouc) est le lieu où se déroule le sabbat. Aker était le maître de cérémonie dans ces soirées orgiaques avec les sorcières (sorginak).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akelarre atau Akerlanda (\"padang kambing\") adalah tempat sabat, dan Aker memimpin pesta-pesta itu bersama para penyihir (sorginak).",
        "en": "Akelarre or Akerlanda ('goat's meadow') is where the sabbath is held, and Aker presided over these revels with the witches (sorginak)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c13",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s3",
      "quote": "En el cristianismo, Akerbeltz fue identificado con el demonio que presidía los aquelarres o reuniones de las brujas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, dalam agama Kristen Akerbeltz disamakan dengan setan yang memimpin akelarre, pertemuan para penyihir.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, Christianity identified Akerbeltz with the devil presiding over the akelarres, the witches' gatherings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akerbeltz-c14",
      "source_id": "akerbeltz-s1",
      "quote": "In words of other inquisitors, Akerbeltz was similar to an enormous dog or to a big ox which is incorrect or lies. This last case could be related to the myth of the Aatxe (another creature of the Basque mythology).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Inkuisitor lain menggambarkannya mirip anjing raksasa atau lembu besar, yang mungkin terkait mitos Aatxe.",
        "en": "Other inquisitors likened him to an enormous dog or a big ox, perhaps related to the myth of the Aatxe."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Sifat Akerbeltz",
        "en": "Nature of Akerbeltz"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Dewa kuno pelindung hewan.",
            "en": "An ancient deity protecting animals."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

            "akerbeltz-c06"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Genius jahat bawah tanah, disamakan dengan setan.",
            "en": "An evil underground genie, equated with the devil."
          },

          ]
        }
      ]

  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anjana adalah peri baik dari mitologi Cantabria yang melindungi hutan, hewan, dan orang yang menderita, melawan raksasa Ojáncanu, dan membawa hadiah bagi anak miskin pada malam 5 Januari.",
    "en": "The anjana is a good fairy of Cantabrian mythology who protects forests, animals and the suffering, opposes the giant Ojáncanu and brings gifts to poor children on the night of 5 January.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c01",
      "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c04",
      "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anjana, dari kata lama jana, \"penyihir\", adalah salah satu peri paling terkenal dalam mitologi Cantabria, dicatat Manuel Llano pada awal abad ke-20. Mereka berkulit putih, berkepang panjang berhias pita sutra, bermahkota bunga liar, berjubah biru, dan membawa tongkat yang berganti warna setiap hari. Sebagian menyebut mereka utusan Tuhan yang kembali ke surga setelah 400 tahun, yang lain roh pohon penjaga hutan; mereka juga dapat berubah wujud.",
      "en": "The anjana, from the old word jana, 'witch', is one of the best-known fairies of Cantabrian mythology, recorded by Manuel Llano in the early 20th century. They are white-skinned, with long silk-ribboned braids, a crown of wild flowers and a blue cape, and carry a staff that changes colour each day. Some call them God's envoys who return to heaven after 400 years, others tree spirits who tend the forests; they can also change shape.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c02",
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c03",
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c07",
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c08",
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Anjana berbicara dengan air mata air, menolong hewan, pohon, kekasih, orang tersesat, dan orang miskin, serta menggagalkan Ojáncanu yang kejam. Mereka memiliki istana dan harta di gua bawah tanah, menari pada malam ekuinoks musim semi sambil menaburkan mawar, dan setiap empat tahun membawa hadiah bagi anak miskin pada malam 5 Januari. Ada juga anjana jahat, tetapi kekuatannya dinetralkan anjana baik. Mereka mirip xana Asturias dan lamia Basque.",
      "en": "Anjanas talk with spring water, help animals, trees, lovers, the lost and the poor, and foil the cruel Ojáncanu. They keep palaces and treasure in underground caves, dance on the spring equinox night scattering roses, and every four years bring gifts to poor children on the night of 5 January. There are also wicked anjanas, but the good ones neutralise them. They resemble the xanas of Asturias and the Basque lamias.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c01",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "are one of the best-known fairies of Cantabrian mythology. These female fairy creatures foil the cruel and ruthless Ojáncanu. In most stories, they are the good fairies of Cantabria, generous and protective of all people.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anjana adalah salah satu peri paling terkenal dalam mitologi Cantabria, peri perempuan yang menggagalkan Ojáncanu yang kejam, dan dalam kebanyakan cerita peri baik yang murah hati dan melindungi semua orang.",
        "en": "The anjanas are among the best-known fairies of Cantabrian mythology, female fairies who foil the cruel Ojáncanu and in most tales are good, generous fairies protecting everyone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Some say they are heavenly beings sent by God to do good deeds, and they go back to heaven after 400 years, never to return. Others, however, indicate that they are spirits of trees who take care of the forests.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang menyebut mereka makhluk surgawi utusan Tuhan untuk berbuat baik yang kembali ke surga setelah 400 tahun, sementara yang lain menyebut mereka roh pohon penjaga hutan.",
        "en": "Some say they are heavenly beings sent by God to do good who return to heaven after 400 years, while others call them tree spirits who tend the forests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "They wear long, jet black or golden braids, adorned with multicolored silk bows and ribbons; a beautiful crown of wild flowers on their head; and a blue cape on a long thin white tunic, and carry in their hands a stick of wicker or hawthorn which shines in a different color every day of the week.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka berkepang panjang hitam legam atau keemasan berhias pita sutra warna-warni, bermahkota bunga liar, bertunik putih panjang dan berjubah biru, dan membawa tongkat rotan atau hawthorn yang bersinar dengan warna berbeda setiap hari.",
        "en": "They wear long jet-black or golden braids with coloured silk ribbons, a crown of wild flowers, a long white tunic and blue cape, and carry a wicker or hawthorn staff that shines a different colour each day of the week."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "They are able to talk with the water that flows from the sources and springs. They help injured animals and trees damaged by storms or Ojáncanu, lovers, people who lose their way in the forest, and the poor and suffering.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dapat berbicara dengan air dari mata air, dan menolong hewan yang terluka, pohon yang dirusak badai atau Ojáncanu, para kekasih, orang yang tersesat di hutan, serta orang miskin dan menderita.",
        "en": "They can talk with the water of springs, and help injured animals, trees damaged by storms or the Ojáncanu, lovers, people lost in the forest, and the poor and suffering."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Traditions state that at night during the spring equinox, they gather in the fells and dance until dawn holding hands and scattering roses. Anyone who manages to find a rose with purple, green, blue, or golden petals will be happy until the time of their death.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam ekuinoks musim semi mereka berkumpul di bukit dan menari sampai fajar sambil bergandengan tangan dan menaburkan mawar; siapa pun yang menemukan mawar berkelopak ungu, hijau, biru, atau emas akan bahagia sampai mati.",
        "en": "On the night of the spring equinox they gather on the fells and dance till dawn holding hands and scattering roses; whoever finds a rose with purple, green, blue or golden petals will be happy until death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Anjanas come to villages of the region during the night of January 5, with the intention of bringing children a variety of toys and gifts. This occurs every four years, generally to poor families",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anjana datang ke desa-desa pada malam 5 Januari membawa mainan dan hadiah untuk anak-anak, setiap empat tahun sekali, biasanya kepada keluarga miskin.",
        "en": "Anjanas come to the villages on the night of 5 January bringing toys and gifts for children, every four years, usually to poor families."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "es un personaje fantástico, referido por el costumbrista Manuel Llano en el primer tercio del s. XX como parte de la mitología cántabra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, anjana adalah tokoh fantastis yang dicatat penulis adat Manuel Llano pada sepertiga awal abad ke-20 sebagai bagian mitologi Cantabria.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the anjana is a fantastic figure recorded by the folklore writer Manuel Llano in the early 20th century as part of Cantabrian mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Tienen una piel blanquísima y una mirada amorosa y serena, pero pueden convertirse en personas, árboles, animales u objetos inanimados.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka berkulit sangat putih dan bertatapan penuh kasih, tetapi dapat berubah menjadi manusia, pohon, hewan, atau benda mati.",
        "en": "They have very white skin and a loving, serene gaze, but can turn into people, trees, animals or objects."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "y poseen grandes palacios subterráneos, ocultos en torcas y cuevas -frecuentemente en fuentes y ríos-, en donde guardan magníficos tesoros",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka memiliki istana bawah tanah yang tersembunyi di lubang dan gua, sering di mata air dan sungai, tempat mereka menyimpan harta yang megah.",
        "en": "They own great underground palaces hidden in sinkholes and caves, often at springs and rivers, where they keep magnificent treasures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Pero también hay anjanas malvadas, si bien su poder suele ser neutralizado por las bondadosas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun ada juga anjana jahat, meski kekuatannya biasanya dinetralkan oleh anjana yang baik.",
        "en": "But there are also wicked anjanas, though their power is usually neutralised by the good ones."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c11",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Bu qadın pərilər qəddar və zalım Oxankanunun qarşısında duran xeyirxah varlıqlardır.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Azerbaijan juga menyebut peri-peri perempuan ini makhluk baik yang melawan Ojáncanu yang kejam.",
        "en": "The Azerbaijani article also calls these female fairies benevolent beings opposing the cruel Ojáncanu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c12",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "from jana, a former word for witches during the Middle Ages",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari jana, kata lama untuk penyihir pada Abad Pertengahan.",
        "en": "The name comes from jana, an old word for witches in the Middle Ages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-c13",
      "source_id": "anjana-cantabrian-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Their depiction in the Cantabrian mythology is reminiscent of the lamias in ancient Greek mythology, as well as the xanas in Asturias, the janas in León, and the lamias in Basque Country, the latter without the zoomorphic appearance.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambaran mereka mengingatkan pada lamia Yunani kuno, xana di Asturias, jana di León, dan lamia Basque, tetapi tanpa ciri hewan.",
        "en": "Their depiction recalls the lamias of ancient Greece, the xanas of Asturias, the janas of León and the Basque lamias, though without animal features."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Apalpador adalah raksasa pembuat arang dari tradisi Natal Galisia yang turun dari pegunungan pada malam Natal atau Tahun Baru untuk meraba perut anak-anak dan meninggalkan kastanye.",
    "en": "The Apalpador is a giant charcoal-burner of Galician Christmas tradition who comes down from the mountains on Christmas or New Year's Eve to feel children's bellies and leave chestnuts.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Apalpador, Apalpabarrigas, atau Pandigueiro adalah raksasa pembuat arang berpakaian compang-camping yang tinggal di pegunungan Galisia timur seperti Courel dan Terra de Trives. Pada malam Natal atau Tahun Baru ia turun dan masuk ke rumah, meraba perut anak-anak untuk melihat apakah mereka makan cukup, lalu meninggalkan kastanye, kadang hadiah, dan mendoakan tahun yang penuh makanan. Malam itu disebut noite de apalpadoiro, dan ada pula versi perempuannya, Pandigueira.",
      "en": "The Apalpador, Apalpabarrigas or Pandigueiro is a ragged giant charcoal-burner who lives in the eastern Galician mountains such as Courel and Terra de Trives. On Christmas or New Year's Eve he comes down and enters houses, feels children's bellies to see if they have eaten enough, then leaves chestnuts, sometimes a present, and wishes them a year full of food. The night is called noite de apalpadoiro, and there is also a female version, the Pandigueira.",
