  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c01",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s1",
      "quote": "Apalpador, Apalpabarrigas o Pandigueiro es un personaje típico de tradición navideña gallega, especialmente en las comarcas de Sarria, Quiroga, Lemos, Tierra de Trives y Los Ancares.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, Apalpador, Apalpabarrigas, atau Pandigueiro adalah tokoh khas tradisi Natal Galisia, terutama di Sarria, Quiroga, Lemos, Tierra de Trives, dan Los Ancares.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the Apalpador, Apalpabarrigas or Pandigueiro is a typical figure of Galician Christmas tradition, especially in Sarria, Quiroga, Lemos, Tierra de Trives and Los Ancares."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c02",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s1",
      "quote": "Se trata de un carbonero con vestuario andrajoso que en Nochebuena o Nochevieja entra en las casas, visita a los niños, les palpa la barriga y, si no está llena, les deja un puñado de castañas. El resto del año vive en los montes orientales de Galicia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia pembuat arang berpakaian compang-camping yang pada malam Natal atau malam Tahun Baru masuk ke rumah, meraba perut anak-anak, dan bila tidak kenyang meninggalkan segenggam kastanye; sepanjang tahun ia tinggal di pegunungan timur Galisia.",
        "en": "He is a ragged charcoal-burner who on Christmas Eve or New Year's Eve enters houses, feels the children's bellies and, if they are not full, leaves a handful of chestnuts; the rest of the year he lives in the eastern mountains of Galicia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c03",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s1",
      "quote": "la existencia del ritual propiciatorio de palpar la barriga de los niños en Nochevieja, que recibe así el nombre de noite de apalpadoiro (noche de palpar).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejarawan Xesús Taboada Chivite mencatat ritual meraba perut anak-anak pada malam Tahun Baru yang disebut noite de apalpadoiro, \"malam meraba\".",
        "en": "The historian Xesús Taboada Chivite recorded the rite of feeling children's bellies on New Year's Eve, called noite de apalpadoiro, 'night of feeling'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c04",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s1",
      "quote": "Hay algunas tonadas y canciones populares relacionadas con este personaje que han llegado hasta nuestros días y que son cantadas en las noites de apalpadoiro para advertir a los pequeños de la llegada inminente del Apalpador y de la necesidad de que se vayan a la cama.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa lagu rakyat tentang Apalpador masih dinyanyikan pada noite de apalpadoiro untuk memberi tahu anak-anak bahwa Apalpador akan datang dan mereka harus tidur.",
        "en": "Folk songs about the Apalpador are still sung on the noites de apalpadoiro to warn children that he is coming and they must go to bed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c05",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s2",
      "quote": "O Apalpador, Apalpa-Barrigas ou Pandigueiro é a figura mítica de um gigante carvoeiro que, segundo a tradição do Natal galego, mora nas montanhas do leste da Galiza (o Courel, Terra de Trives, Bierzo)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Portugis, Apalpador adalah raksasa pembuat arang yang menurut tradisi Natal Galisia tinggal di pegunungan Galisia timur (Courel, Terra de Trives, Bierzo).",
        "en": "According to the Portuguese article, the Apalpador is a giant charcoal-burner who by Galician Christmas tradition lives in the mountains of eastern Galicia (Courel, Terra de Trives, Bierzo)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c06",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s2",
      "quote": "deixando-lhes um montezinho de castanhas, eventualmente algum presente e desejando-lhes que tenham um ano vindouro cheio de felicidade e de alimento.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia meninggalkan setumpuk kecil kastanye, kadang hadiah, dan mendoakan tahun mendatang yang penuh kebahagiaan dan makanan.",
        "en": "He leaves a little heap of chestnuts, sometimes a present, and wishes them a coming year full of happiness and food."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c07",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s2",
      "quote": "Na zona falante de galego no Bierzo, paróquia de Santa Maria de Trives e nos Ancares em Leão também existe a figura da Pandigueira, Apalpadoira ou Apalpabarrigas em versão mulher.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di daerah berbahasa Galisia di Bierzo, Trives, dan Ancares di León juga ada versi perempuannya, Pandigueira atau Apalpadoira.",
        "en": "In the Galician-speaking parts of Bierzo, Trives and the Ancares of León there is also a female version, the Pandigueira or Apalpadoira."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c08",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s2",
      "quote": "O trabalho de recuperação desta figura começou em 2006 através da obra de José André Lôpez Gonçâlez, publicada através do Portal Galego da Língua vinculado com a Associaçom Galega da Língua.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Upaya menghidupkan kembali tokoh ini dimulai pada 2006 lewat karya José André Lôpez Gonçâlez di Portal Galego da Língua.",
        "en": "Efforts to revive the figure began in 2006 with the work of José André Lôpez Gonçâlez on the Portal Galego da Língua."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c09",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s2",
      "quote": "Um ano depois, a associação cultural compostelana A Gentalha do Pichel anunciou que iria começar uma promoção desta figura, visando a sua plena recuperação e normalização no âmbito do natal na Galiza",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setahun kemudian, perkumpulan budaya A Gentalha do Pichel dari Santiago de Compostela mulai mempromosikannya agar kembali menjadi bagian Natal Galisia.",
        "en": "A year later the Compostela cultural association A Gentalha do Pichel began promoting him to restore him fully to the Galician Christmas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c10",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s3",
      "quote": "L'apalpador, apalpa-barrigas u pandigueiro ye una figura d'a mitolochía gallega d'un chigant carbonero que vive en as montanyas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Aragon, Apalpador adalah tokoh mitologi Galisia berupa raksasa pembuat arang yang tinggal di pegunungan.",
        "en": "According to the Aragonese article, the Apalpador is a figure of Galician mythology, a giant charcoal-burner living in the mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalpador-c11",
      "source_id": "apalpador-s3",
      "quote": "l'apalpador baixa la nueit d'o 25 d'aviento u a nueit de cabo d'anyo",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Aragon menyebut ia turun pada malam 25 Desember atau malam akhir tahun.",
        "en": "The Aragonese article says he comes down on the night of 25 December or on New Year's Eve."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dip adalah anjing vampir hitam dan pincang dari mitos Katalan, utusan Iblis yang mengisap darah ternak dan pemabuk pada malam hari di sekitar desa Pratdip.",
    "en": "The dip is a black, lame vampire dog of Catalan myth, the Devil's emissary that sucks the blood of cattle and drunkards at night around the village of Pratdip.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dip-catalan-myth-c01",
      "dip-catalan-myth-c03"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dip adalah anjing neraka hitam berbulu dari mitos Katalan, utusan Iblis yang pincang sebelah kaki dan mengisap darah. Menurut legenda, anjing-anjing ini keluar malam hari mengisap darah ternak dan para pemabuk yang pulang dari kedai minum, sehingga legenda ini diduga untuk menakuti pemabuk. Gambarnya sudah ada pada altar Santa Marina de Pratdip tahun 1602 dan pada lambang desa Pratdip.",
      "en": "The dip is a hairy black hellhound of Catalan myth, the Devil's lame emissary that sucks blood. By legend these dogs went out at night to suck the blood of cattle and of drunkards leaving the taverns, so the legend was probably meant to frighten drinkers. They appear on the Santa Marina de Pratdip altarpiece of 1602 and on Pratdip's coat of arms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dip-catalan-myth-c01",
        "dip-catalan-myth-c02",

    {
      "id": "Menurut tradisi nama Pratdip berasal dari anjing-anjing ini, yang menghilang pada abad ke-19, dan dip konon dari kata Arab untuk serigala; artikel bahasa Katalan menyebut itu etimologi rakyat bagi nama pra-Romawi, dan tidak ada saksi terdokumentasi bahwa legendanya benar-benar diceritakan di desa. Joan Perucho memakai dip dalam novel Les històries naturals (1960).",
      "en": "By tradition Pratdip is named after these dogs, which vanished in the 19th century, and dip supposedly comes from Arabic for wolf; the Catalan article calls this folk etymology for a pre-Roman name, and there is no documented witness that the legend was really told in the village.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c01",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "is an evil, black hellhound and emissary of the Devil, who sucks people's blood. Like other figures associated with demons in Catalan myth, he is lame in one leg. Dip is pictured on the escutcheon of Pratdip.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos Katalan, Dip adalah anjing neraka hitam yang jahat, utusan Iblis yang mengisap darah manusia; seperti tokoh iblis Katalan lain ia pincang sebelah kaki, dan gambarnya ada pada lambang Pratdip.",
        "en": "In Catalan myth the Dip is an evil black hellhound, the Devil's emissary, who sucks people's blood; like other Catalan demonic figures he is lame in one leg, and he appears on the arms of Pratdip."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c02",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "The legend is very old. Images of these vampire dogs already appear on the altarpiece of Santa Marina de Pratdip, from 1602. They also appear in another altarpiece from 1730, cut out on a gold background.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini sangat tua: gambar anjing-anjing vampir ini sudah ada pada altar Santa Marina de Pratdip tahun 1602 dan altar lain tahun 1730 berlatar emas.",
        "en": "The legend is very old: images of these vampire dogs appear on the Santa Marina de Pratdip altarpiece of 1602 and another of 1730 on a gold ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c03",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "Legend has it that these dogs sucked the blood of cattle, but only went out at night, and among their victims were drunken night owls who went to drink wine in the village taverns.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, anjing-anjing ini mengisap darah ternak, hanya keluar malam hari, dan korbannya termasuk para pemabuk yang pulang malam dari kedai minum desa.",
        "en": "By legend these dogs sucked cattle's blood, went out only at night, and their victims included drunken night-owls leaving the village taverns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c04",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed that this legend was intended to frighten the drunkards of the village and thus prevent them from engaging in more drinking.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini diduga dimaksudkan untuk menakuti pemabuk desa agar berhenti minum.",
        "en": "The legend is thought to have been meant to frighten village drunkards out of further drinking."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c05",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "According to tradition, the name of the village comes from these dogs, which are said to have disappeared in the nineteenth century. At the entrance to Pratdip there is a monument to this mythological being, but as it is depicted it has a very kind image.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tradisi, nama desa Pratdip berasal dari anjing-anjing ini, yang konon menghilang pada abad ke-19, dan di pintu masuk desa ada monumen dip yang tampak ramah.",
        "en": "By tradition the village's name comes from these dogs, said to have vanished in the 19th century, and a friendly-looking dip monument stands at the entrance to Pratdip."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c06",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s1",
      "quote": "Due to his thirst for blood, the dip served to inspire Joan Perucho who, in his novel The Natural Stories (1960), tells the story of Onofre de Dip, a vampire with the ability to transform himself into many animals.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dip mengilhami Joan Perucho dalam novel Les històries naturals (1960) tentang Onofre de Dip, vampir yang dapat berubah menjadi banyak hewan.",
        "en": "The dip inspired Joan Perucho's novel Les històries naturals (1960) about Onofre de Dip, a vampire who can turn into many animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c07",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s2",
      "quote": "El nombre del pueblo proviene de la palabra dip (Pratdip = Prado de dips). Dip proviene del árabe y su significado es lobo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, nama desa berasal dari kata dip (Pratdip = \"padang dip\"), dan dip berasal dari bahasa Arab yang berarti serigala.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the village name comes from dip (Pratdip = 'meadow of dips'), and dip comes from Arabic for wolf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c08",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s2",
      "quote": "No hay pruebas fehacientes, ni testigos documentados de que esta leyenda se contara en el pueblo",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak ada bukti kuat atau saksi terdokumentasi bahwa legenda ini memang diceritakan di desa itu.",
        "en": "There is no firm evidence or documented witness that this legend was actually told in the village."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c09",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s3",
      "quote": "Segons l'etimologia popular, el nom del poble vindria d'aquests éssers mitològics, que es diu que van desaparèixer al segle xix (la realitat, però, és que és un topònim d'origen preromà).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Katalan, anggapan bahwa nama desa berasal dari dip hanyalah etimologi rakyat; sebenarnya Pratdip adalah nama tempat pra-Romawi.",
        "en": "According to the Catalan article, deriving the village name from the dips is folk etymology; Pratdip is actually a pre-Roman place name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dip-catalan-myth-c10",
      "source_id": "dip-catalan-myth-s3",
      "quote": "És una mena de gos vampir, malvat i pelut, un emissari del dimoni que, com tants d'altres, és coix d'una cama. S'alimenta de la sang d'animals i persones.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dip adalah semacam anjing vampir yang jahat dan berbulu, utusan setan yang pincang sebelah kaki, dan memakan darah hewan dan manusia.",
        "en": "The dip is a kind of evil, hairy vampire dog, a devil's emissary lame in one leg, feeding on the blood of animals and people."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Doñas de fuera, \"Nona-nona dari Luar\", adalah makhluk peri dalam cerita rakyat Sisilia yang membawa perempuan ke pertemuan malam di Benevento dan muncul dalam pengadilan penyihir abad ke-16 dan ke-17.",
    "en": "The Doñas de fuera, 'Ladies from the Outside', are fairy beings of Sicilian folklore who carried women to night gatherings at Benevento and appear in the 16th- and 17th-century witch trials.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "donas-de-fuera-c01",
      "donas-de-fuera-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Doñas de fuera adalah makhluk peri dalam cerita rakyat Sisilia, sebanding dengan peri Inggris, yang namanya juga dipakai untuk perempuan yang bergaul dengan mereka. Mereka cantik, berpakaian putih, merah, atau hitam, berkaki cakar kucing atau kuku kuda, datang dalam kelompok lima atau tujuh diiringi kecapi, dan membawa perempuan \"berdarah manis\" ke Benevento dengan kambing terbang. Setiap Selasa, Kamis, dan Sabtu mereka bertemu anggota kelompoknya di hutan, dan kelompok Tujuh Peri dapat berubah menjadi kucing.",
      "en": "The Doñas de fuera are fairy beings of Sicilian folklore, comparable to English fairies, whose name was also used for the women who kept company with them. Beautiful, dressed in white, red or black, with cat's paws or horse's hooves for feet, they came in groups of five or seven to lute music and carried 'sweet-blooded' women to Benevento on flying goats. Every Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday they met their company in the woods, and the Seven Fairies could turn into cats.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "donas-de-fuera-c01",
        "donas-de-fuera-c02",
        "donas-de-fuera-c03",
        "donas-de-fuera-c04",
        "donas-de-fuera-c05",
        "donas-de-fuera-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka mudah tersinggung: lelaki yang memukul atau tersandung peri yang tak terlihat menderita kram. Pada malam hari mereka berkeliling rumah, makan, minum, dan memeluk bayi. Antara akhir abad ke-16 dan pertengahan abad ke-17, 65 orang di Sisilia, banyak di antaranya dianggap rekan para peri, diadili Inkuisisi karena sihir.",
      "en": "They were easily offended: a man who struck or tripped over an unseen fairy got a cramp. By night they went from house to house, eating, drinking and embracing infants.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c01",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "Doñas de fuera (Spanish for \"Ladies from the Outside\"; Sicily was under Spanish rule at the time) were supernatural female beings comparable to the fairies of English folklore. In the 16th to mid-17th centuries, the doñas de fuera also played a role in the witch trials in Sicily.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Sisilia, Doñas de fuera (bahasa Spanyol, \"Nona-nona dari Luar\", karena Sisilia saat itu di bawah Spanyol) adalah makhluk gaib perempuan yang sebanding dengan peri Inggris, dan pada abad ke-16 sampai pertengahan abad ke-17 berperan dalam pengadilan penyihir di Sisilia.",
        "en": "In Sicilian folklore the Doñas de fuera (Spanish, 'Ladies from the Outside', as Sicily was then under Spain) were supernatural female beings like English fairies, and from the 16th to mid-17th centuries they figured in Sicily's witch trials."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c02",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "would make contact with humans, mostly women deemed to have “sweet blood”, whom they took to Benevento ... by mounting them on magical, flying goats.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menghubungi manusia, kebanyakan perempuan yang dianggap \"berdarah manis\", dan membawa mereka ke Benevento dengan menunggang kambing terbang ajaib.",
        "en": "They contacted humans, mostly women thought to have 'sweet blood', and carried them to Benevento on magical flying goats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c03",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "They were described as beauties dressed in white, red or black; they could be male or female, and their feet were the paws of cats, horses or of a peculiar \"round\" shape. They came in groups of five or seven and a male fairy played the lute or the guitar while dancing.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka digambarkan cantik dan berpakaian putih, merah, atau hitam, bisa laki-laki atau perempuan, dengan kaki seperti cakar kucing, kuku kuda, atau berbentuk \"bulat\", dan datang dalam kelompok lima atau tujuh sementara peri laki-laki bermain kecapi atau gitar sambil menari.",
        "en": "They were beauties in white, red or black, male or female, with feet like cat's paws, horse's hooves or a strange 'round' shape, coming in groups of five or seven while a male fairy played lute or guitar and danced."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c04",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "Every Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday, the fairies met the humans belonging to their company in the woods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setiap Selasa, Kamis, dan Sabtu para peri bertemu manusia anggota kelompok mereka di hutan.",
        "en": "Every Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday the fairies met the humans of their company in the woods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c05",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "A congregation called The Seven Fairies could transform themselves to cats and something called aydon; ayodons were able to kill.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelompok bernama Tujuh Peri dapat berubah menjadi kucing dan makhluk bernama aydon yang mampu membunuh.",
        "en": "A congregation called the Seven Fairies could turn into cats and into 'aydons', which could kill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c06",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "In one story, a man who was not associated with the fairies and was unable to see them developed a painful cramp after hitting one of the fairies who was listening to him play music.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah, seorang lelaki yang tidak bisa melihat para peri menderita kram yang menyakitkan setelah memukul peri yang sedang mendengarkan musiknya.",
        "en": "In one story a man who could not see the fairies suffered a painful cramp after striking a fairy who was listening to him play."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c07",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "Another story involves several people who had disturbed the fairies while they nocturnally travelled from house to house, eating and drinking as they routinely embraced the town's infants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menceritakan orang-orang yang mengganggu para peri saat mereka berkeliling dari rumah ke rumah pada malam hari, makan, minum, dan memeluk bayi-bayi kota.",
        "en": "Another tells of people who disturbed the fairies as they went from house to house by night, eating, drinking and embracing the town's infants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c08",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s1",
      "quote": "The trial summaries, sent to the Spanish Inquisition's Suprema in Madrid by the Sicilian tribunal, reflected a total of 65 people, eight of them male, many of whom were believed to be associates of fairies, who were put on trial for sorcery.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ringkasan pengadilan yang dikirim tribunal Sisilia ke Suprema Inkuisisi Spanyol di Madrid mencatat 65 orang, delapan di antaranya laki-laki, banyak yang dianggap rekan para peri, yang diadili karena sihir.",
        "en": "Trial summaries sent by the Sicilian tribunal to the Spanish Inquisition's Suprema in Madrid record 65 people, eight of them men, many believed to be fairy associates, tried for sorcery."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c09",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s2",
      "quote": "Les procès des sorcières féeriques de Sicile se sont déroulés de la fin du XVIe siècle au milieu du XVIIe siècle. Ils représentent un phénomène unique, car dans cette région, les procès de sorcières impliquèrent le folklore des fées.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, pengadilan \"penyihir peri\" Sisilia berlangsung dari akhir abad ke-16 sampai pertengahan abad ke-17 dan unik karena melibatkan cerita rakyat tentang peri.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the Sicilian 'fairy witch' trials ran from the late 16th to the mid-17th century and were unique in involving fairy folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c10",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s2",
      "quote": "Dans une histoire, un homme sans rapport avec elles, et ne les voyant donc pas, souffrit d'une crampe douloureuse après avoir trébuché sur une d'elles.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi artikel bahasa Prancis, lelaki itu kram setelah tersandung salah satu peri.",
        "en": "In the French article's version, the man got the cramp after tripping over one of the fairies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "donas-de-fuera-c11",
      "source_id": "donas-de-fuera-s3",
      "quote": "erano esseri femminili soprannaturali paragonabili alle fate del folclore britannico.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, Donas de fuera adalah makhluk gaib perempuan yang sebanding dengan peri dalam cerita rakyat Britania.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, the Donas de fuera were supernatural female beings comparable to the fairies of British folklore."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gaueko adalah roh malam dalam mitologi Basque yang melarang manusia bekerja di luar rumah pada malam hari dan menghukum orang yang pamer tidak takut gelap.",
    "en": "Gaueko is the spirit of the night in Basque mythology who forbids people to work outside at night and punishes those who boast of not fearing the dark.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gaueko-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gaueko, \"yang dari malam\", adalah roh atau dewa malam Basque yang menguasai waktu dari tengah malam sampai kokok ayam pertama. Ia melarang manusia meninggalkan rumah dan bekerja di luar pada malam hari, dan menghukum orang yang pamer keberanian; pelanggar bisa diculik atau hilang. Ia hadir sebagai embusan angin sambil mengucapkan \"malam untuk Gaueko, siang untuk yang siang\".",
      "en": "Gaueko, 'of the night', is the Basque spirit or god of the night who rules from midnight to the first cockcrow. He forbids people to leave home and work outside at night and punishes boasters; transgressors may be abducted or vanish. He makes himself felt as a gust of wind, saying 'the night for Gaueko, the day for the day'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gaueko-c01",
        "gaueko-c03",
        "gaueko-c05",
        "gaueko-c06",
        "gaueko-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dianggap setan atau dewa pagan, dan muncul tak terlihat, sebagai serigala hitam yang kadang berdiri, sapi, burung hantu, domba, atau monster. Seperti Mari ia dapat berubah wujud dan menguasai unsur alam, tetapi tak berdaya melawannya; ketika manusia meminta tolong, Mari memberi cahaya bulan, Ilargi.",
      "en": "He is seen as a devil or pagan god, appearing invisibly or as a black wolf sometimes standing upright, a cow, owl, sheep or monster. Like Mari he can change shape and command the elements, but he is powerless against her; when people asked for help, Mari gave them the light of the moon, Ilargi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gaueko-c02",
        "gaueko-c04",
        "gaueko-c08",
        "gaueko-c09",
        "gaueko-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c01",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s1",
      "quote": "In Basque mythology, Gaueko is the spirit of the night. He does not allow humans to do certain works outside the house during the night. He especially punishes all those who try to show off as brave in the night, boasting of not fearing the darkness.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Basque, Gaueko adalah roh malam yang melarang manusia melakukan pekerjaan tertentu di luar rumah pada malam hari, dan terutama menghukum orang yang pamer keberanian dengan mengaku tidak takut gelap.",
        "en": "In Basque mythology Gaueko is the spirit of the night, who forbids certain work outside the house at night and especially punishes those who boast of not fearing the dark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c02",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s1",
      "quote": "He is considered to be a devil in some accounts, a jentil or gentilic divinity in others.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian kisah ia dianggap setan, dalam kisah lain jentil atau dewa pagan.",
        "en": "Some accounts make him a devil, others a jentil or pagan deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c03",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s1",
      "quote": "In some cases he makes his presence felt as a gust of wind, as he pronounces these words: Gaua Gauekoarentzat, eguna egunezkoarentzat",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia hadir sebagai embusan angin sambil berkata, \"Gaua Gauekoarentzat, eguna egunezkoarentzat\" (malam untuk Gaueko, siang untuk yang siang).",
        "en": "Sometimes he makes himself felt as a gust of wind, saying 'Gaua Gauekoarentzat, eguna egunezkoarentzat' (the night for Gaueko, the day for the day)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c04",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s1",
      "quote": "Sometimes he appears in the shape of a cow, sometimes in that of a monster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia muncul berwujud sapi, kadang berwujud monster.",
        "en": "Sometimes he appears as a cow, sometimes as a monster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c05",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s1",
      "quote": "In Basque, Gaueko literally means \"of the night\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Basque, Gaueko berarti \"yang dari malam\".",
        "en": "In Basque, Gaueko literally means 'of the night'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c06",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s2",
      "quote": "Elle est généralement comprise comme la période allant de minuit jusqu'au premier chant du coq, bien qu'elle puisse également être délimitée par les angélus du soir et du matin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, malam yang dikuasai Gaueko biasanya dipahami dari tengah malam sampai kokok ayam pertama, meski bisa juga dibatasi lonceng Angelus sore dan pagi.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Gaueko's night is usually understood as from midnight to the first cockcrow, though it may also be bounded by the evening and morning Angelus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c07",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s2",
      "quote": "Ce règne s'accompagne d'une loi interdisant aux humains de quitter la protection du foyer, considéré comme un espace protégé par les esprits familiaux. La transgression de cette règle expose les individus à des risques tels que l'enlèvement ou la disparition inexpliquée.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kekuasaannya disertai larangan bagi manusia meninggalkan perlindungan rumah, yang dijaga roh-roh keluarga; pelanggarnya berisiko diculik atau hilang secara misterius.",
        "en": "His rule comes with a ban on leaving the protection of the home, guarded by household spirits; those who break it risk abduction or unexplained disappearance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c08",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s3",
      "quote": "Se le representaba como una presencia invisible, un lobo negro (otsobeltza u otsobaltza) –a veces alzado sobre dos patas– u otro tipo de animal como vacas, lechuzas u ovejas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, ia digambarkan sebagai kehadiran tak terlihat, serigala hitam (otsobeltza) yang kadang berdiri dengan dua kaki, atau hewan lain seperti sapi, burung hantu, atau domba.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, he was pictured as an invisible presence, a black wolf (otsobeltza) sometimes standing on two legs, or another animal such as a cow, owl or sheep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c09",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s3",
      "quote": "Gaueko, al igual que la diosa Mari, sería capaz de cambiar de apariencia y dominar los elementos, aunque impotente contra ella. Se decía que comía pastores y ovejas, y por ello se le temía.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seperti dewi Mari, Gaueko dapat berubah wujud dan menguasai unsur alam, tetapi tak berdaya melawan Mari, dan ia ditakuti karena konon memakan gembala dan domba.",
        "en": "Like the goddess Mari, Gaueko could change shape and command the elements, though powerless against her, and he was feared for eating shepherds and sheep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaueko-c10",
      "source_id": "gaueko-s3",
      "quote": "Ella les bendijo con la luz de su primera hija, Ilargi (la luna), pero su luz era insuficiente y los humanos volvieron a pedirle ayuda",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika manusia meminta pertolongan Mari terhadap Gaueko, ia memberkati mereka dengan cahaya putri pertamanya, Ilargi (bulan), tetapi cahaya itu tidak cukup sehingga mereka meminta lagi.",
        "en": "When people asked Mari for help against Gaueko, she blessed them with the light of her first daughter, Ilargi (the moon), but it was not enough and they asked again."
      }
    }
  ],
